## 2026-11-07 — Live Summary zoom verification and controls layout — 1.0.254 / build 254

After the user unlocked the Mac, verified the installed Summary reader on the Andy project: View shows Zoom In / Zoom Out / Actual Size only on Summary, and they disappear on Transcript. Verified menu Zoom In, Command-Equals, shifted Command-Plus, Command-Minus, and Command-0; slider changes reached 200%. With Preview shown, the slider/percentage disappear and the compact buttons remain. With Preview hidden they return. Live Apple speech and word/sentence highlights continue while zoom changes; pause stays paused when a zoom button is used, and resume works. The 200% screenshot shows wrapped text and highlighted speech within the reading viewport. This supersedes the locked-Mac UI limitation recorded for 1.0.253. Physical trackpad pinch remains untested because this UI tool does not expose a pinch gesture.

Found a clipped reading-status label during live QA. Gave the Mac status/engine slots a zero minimum width and limited the engine label to one line. Installed 1.0.254/build 254 through `script/build_and_run.sh --verify`; the new live 200% reading screenshot confirms the complete status label and all playback/zoom controls fit. Left the existing Summary selected, Preview hidden, zoom 100%, speech stopped. No summary content was edited.

Versions synchronized to Windows 1.0.254/package 1.0.254.0. Build/install log: `build/summary-zoom-layout-install.log`. App, Share and Finder extensions passed version/signing continuity and registered-instance checks with team LLVWNNUV26; packaged Help passed. Native Windows execution remains blocked by the Parallels edition restriction; previous semantic/source tests remain valid for the unchanged Windows feature code. Website publication remains blocked by unauthenticated Cloudflare. No commit/push.

## 2026-11-07 — Summary zoom — 1.0.253 / build 253

Added Summary reader zoom from 50–400% with native Mac trackpad magnification, plus/minus/100% buttons beside Summary playback, and a bottom-right slider/current percentage only while Preview is hidden (normal or OS full-screen editor). View exposes Zoom In, Zoom Out and Actual Size (100%) only on Summary, disabled in editing/processing, with Command-=, Command-Minus and Command-0; shifted Plus is accepted. Zoom stays per open editor and does not write project text or alter exports/video. Fonts/spacing reflow within the viewport; zoom preserves selection and viewport text, and Follow uses the spoken word even in long sentences. Pinch uses its pointer location as the reflow anchor. Slider dragging no longer gets mistaken for text selection.

Windows mirrors commands, bounds, gestures through native ScrollViewer zoom/reflow, Ctrl shortcuts plus shifted/keypad aliases, compact controls and preview-dependent footer. Updated both menu parity specifications/checker and mutation tests; new Windows native smoke coverage checks wrapping, highlight/selection preservation, footer visibility and zoom during speech.

Validation: combined menu checker passes (49 translated editor shortcuts); Mac menu/parity/narration-options suites passed all 47 cases (`build/summary-zoom-final-tests.log`). Both menu suites cover omission and guard/bound drift. `script/test_summary_zoom.py` passed native AppKit layout at 50/100/200/400%, multilingual UTF-16 ranges, highlight and selection preservation, scroll anchor, bounds/steps/reset. `script/test_summary_zoom_speech.py` passed the production readback model with muted native Apple speech: word callbacks continue across zoom, pause remains paused, resume/stop work. Its unused Piper/error factories are stand-ins; no Piper runtime or physical gesture validation is claimed. Xcode diagnostics caught and resolved a CGFloat type ambiguity; packaged Release build/installation passed via `script/build_and_run.sh --verify`.

Installed `/Applications/VidiStudio.app` reports 1.0.253/build 253. Matching Share and Finder extensions retain team LLVWNNUV26 and passed designated-requirement continuity, version, signing, and registered-instance verification. Build product was removed; Spotlight and LaunchServices report only `/Applications/VidiStudio.app` and its two embedded extensions; no build-path copy remains. Packaged Help registration, all 13 pages, topic anchors, internal links, images and search index passed. Canonical Help/editor documentation and generated Windows Help include zoom/shortcuts; website guide and shortcut source/local production build updated. Website TypeScript/build passed with the existing bundle-size warning; local browser verified Zoom search, navigation to chapter 10.5, visible zoom heading and valid documentation anchors.

Limitations: the Mac became locked during UI verification and remained locked after a later check; an unlock request was sent. New controls, physical pinch, and combined live UI/playback were not observed. Native Windows build/install/UI cannot run: the existing Windows 11 VM is stopped and `prlctl start` explicitly requires Parallels Pro or Business. Project editors on Mac and Windows now hide timeline thumbnail images by default. View > Show Thumbnail Images / Hide Thumbnail Images (`toggle-thumbnail-images`) appears immediately after Show/Hide Timeline, with the same idle-editor guard and no new shortcut. The remembered device preference applies across editors, independently of whole-timeline visibility. Mac replaces the image strip with a compact accessible Video Position seek row; Windows collapses the image row and retains the existing Video position slider. Both retain transport, transcript/AD tracks and playheads, avoid loading hidden timeline images, and preserve thumbnail files/project-browser images. Windows propagates this preference to other open editor windows through the existing settings broadcast. Native Windows smoke expectations now exercise hiding/showing, saved preference/title changes, retained seeking and the existing thumbnail-width/playhead checks. The existing shared parity parser already handles this ordinary Button/Command construct; both desktop regression suites now reject omission of the new command on either side. Menu-contract documentation matches both platforms.

## 2026-11-07 — Safari Share extension trust and release verification (1.0.246 / build 246)

Diagnosed the exact macOS “differs from previously opened versions” warning. The initial installed app, Share extension and Finder extension already all reported 1.0.245/build 245 and carried the same valid Apple Development signature/team LLVWNNUV26. Read-only container metadata showed the Share extension's old owner had validationCategory none and no signing team; the app and Finder extension had development owners. System secinitd evidence subsequently confirmed the current Share binary identity was not in that old container ACL and triggered the prompt. This was stale container trust from an earlier unsigned/ad hoc identity, not a stale version or a YouTube-link error. Apple DTS documents this exact alert and stable signing remedy: https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/739988 . No container or privacy data was deleted or edited, and no protections were disabled.

Added script/verify_extensions.py to the packaged build/install path. Before replacing the installed app it checks both extensions' version/build, stable bundle IDs/extension points, strict signatures, project signing team, canonical signed entitlements and satisfaction of the previous app/extensions' designated requirements. Installation stops existing extension processes, unregisters build-path extensions, registers the installed copies and requires exactly one current PlugInKit instance per extension at the installed location. Existing user enable/disable elections are preserved. Unregistering an already-unregistered build extension is benign; final registration verification remains mandatory. Mac AGENTS.md now requires extension updates, signing continuity, registration verification and evidence on every release, and documents safe handling of legacy container approval. Windows AGENTS.md points cross-platform version updates to these Mac requirements.

Validation and installed release:
- Xcode UI verified the project open and external MCP access set to Always. Xcode MCP found the VidiStudio scheme and both extension schemes; BuildProject succeeded in 28.205 seconds with no errors. Removed/unregistered its disposable DerivedData Debug app afterward.
- Required script/build_and_run.sh --verify succeeded, replacing and launching /Applications/VidiStudio.app at 1.0.246/build 246 (final PID 91956). Both embedded extensions report 1.0.246/246 and match the previous designated requirements. Final strict verification and PlugInKit checks pass. Spotlight finds only /Applications/VidiStudio.app; LaunchServices lists that app and its two nested extensions. Final build product removed. Evidence: build/share-extension-install.log, build/codesign.log.
- Four new metadata regression tests cover matching versions, stale marketing/build values for either extension, wrong identity/extension point and missing extension. Final Mac menu/extension suite: 31 pass; additional Mac parity/Finder/extension suite: 15 pass. Windows menu/parity suite: 36 pass using the Mac virtual environment. An initial system-Python Windows parity invocation lacked certifi; rerun in the existing project environment passed. Swift production share-link harness compiles and passes. Two installed packaged-engine CLI checks pass with VIDISTUDIO_TEST_ENGINE set to /Applications/VidiStudio.app/Contents/Resources/Engine/vidistudio-engine. Evidence: build/share-extension-tests.log, build/share-extension-packaged.log, /private/tmp/share-extension-{final-tests,windows-parity}.log.
- Native Safari Share > VidiStudio on the public video Jm0MLlE4x0U successfully created/opened The Fox and the Bird project, with the matching URL and 00:12:07.000 duration visible in the editor. secinitd recorded user consent at 15:55:44 local time; the container now includes the development/team owner. Repeated sharing retained exactly one matching local project. After the final replacement install, the installed Share extension launched again without a new secinitd consent prompt. ## 2026-11-07 — Reuse extracted YouTube soundtracks (1.0.245 / build 245)

YouTube original-audio and described-audio exports now save the first verified soundtrack in `youtube-audio/<videoID>/` inside the portable project. A relative-path sidecar records the canonical video URL and SHA-256; later exports reuse the file after checking its integrity. This works across project reopen/move, narration changes, export-format changes, and mixing failures. Missing/corrupt audio or an invalid sidecar causes a fresh bounded download. Audio-only stream/duration checks run before cache publication; existing project-duration and export validation remain in effect. Atomic publication occurs before mixing, so completed extractions survive failed/cancelled encoding while incomplete downloads/staging files are removed. Generated-content cleanup retains the saved soundtrack. Existing playback/transcription choices and native connection guards are unchanged. Earlier releases deleted temporary extractions, so existing projects need one successful extraction with this release before reuse is possible. The shared cache implementation is mirrored in the Windows engine without replacing its platform-specific downloader/cancellation behavior. No menu changes.

Validation:
- Mac YouTube suite: 14 tests pass, including real FFmpeg mixing, repeated AD exports after narration replacement, MP3/WAV changes, portable reuse after failed encoding/reopen/move/cleanup, missing/corrupt/different-video repair, and invalid/cancelled download rejection. Audio-source/recovery/routing suites: 36 tests pass. Logs: `build/youtube-cache-tests.log`, `build/youtube-cache-audio-tests.log`.
- Installed packaged engine, explicitly selected with `VIDISTUDIO_TEST_ENGINE=/Applications/VidiStudio.app/Contents/Resources/Engine/vidistudio-engine`, ran three CLI exports (original WAV, AD MP3, AD WAV) from one seeded synthetic soundtrack. Every operation reported Using saved YouTube audio, retained its exact checksum, and invoked no downloader. This validates packaged reuse and real media export; first retrieval was mocked and no live YouTube or Gemini request was performed. Evidence: `build/youtube-cache-packaged-check.log`; reproducible fixture `/private/tmp/youtube-cache-packaged-check.py`.
- Both menu parity checkers pass (46 translated editor shortcuts). Mac menu/version/parity suite: 34 pass; Windows: 36 pass. Versions match Mac 1.0.245/build 245 and Windows 1.0.245/package 1.0.245.0. Windows YouTube suite passes 15 tests on this Mac host; original-audio and recovery suites pass 25 and 5 tests. A broader Windows audio test run exposed an unrelated existing C# fixture failure: `test_audio_source_models` omits `NarrationTimeFormat` needed by the current StudioProject model (CS0103). That fixture was left unchanged; no native Windows validation is claimed.
- Xcode MCP confirmed the open project and tool access, but returned no schemes; BuildProject reported No active scheme. Required `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` completed the packaged Release build, signing, replacement installation and launch (PID 85527) after /Applications sandbox escalation. Installed plist reports 1.0.245/245; deep strict signature validation passes. Spotlight lists only `/Applications/VidiStudio.app`; LaunchServices lists that app and its two nested extensions. The duplicate build product was removed. Log: `build/youtube-cache-install.log`.
- Canonical export/privacy Help updated; generation validates 13 pages and internal links, Windows synchronization generates 12 topics, and installed Help passes registration/pages/anchors/links/images/style/search-index checks. Native Help Viewer rendering/search and VoiceOver speech were not exercised.
- Website guide, export/YouTube feature sections and privacy page now explain retained soundtracks and temporary-file cleanup. TypeScript/lint and production build pass (existing bundle-size advisory). Production guide search/navigation passes at desktop/mobile sizes with no runtime errors; log `build/youtube-cache-docs-browser.log`. Sources and local output are updated; live publication is blocked: Wrangler 4.158.0 whoami reports not authenticated (`/private/tmp/youtube-cache-cloudflare.log`).

## 2026-11-06 — Activate completed narration and recover segment audio (1.0.232 / build 232)

Root cause: `language_results.produce` staged every later narration run as a review draft. The successful audio was on disk, but native viewing/project playback loaded the older selected saved descriptions. Both engines now automatically save and select completed `synthesize-tts` results, including runs with per-segment failures. Previous saved versions and their files remain available; failed/cancelled operations still preserve the selected result and retain recoverable drafts. Analysis, text reanalysis and alternative-import review semantics are unchanged. No menu or native UI copy changes.

The user's Dandiya project contained valid 2.155374-second MP3 narration for segment 2 in draft version 8, fitting its 3.30-second slot. Verified its MP3 audio stream and exact text/timing match against the selected version 4, backed up the manifest to `/private/tmp/vidistudio-narration-status-before-recovery.json`, then used the engine's `review-result --disposition keep` to recover/select version 8. Earlier saved results were retained. No description text or timing was edited. Segment 9 has no usable narration: its speech takes about 6.7 seconds for a 6.5-second slot. It still needs shorter wording or a valid longer gap. Native inspection of the installed app confirms AD 2 says Synchronized audio generated with Sangeeta (Enhanced), AD 1–8 have generated status, and only AD 9 says Synchronized audio not generated.

## 2026-11-05 — Delete transcript/description segments despite unrelated saved timing conflicts (1.0.220 / build 220)

Fixed `save-segments` on both desktop engines: unchanged saved transcript rows retain their existing timing, while added/edited rows still require nonempty text, unique IDs, finite positive time ranges inside the video, and no transcript-row overlaps. This matches the existing changed-description validation. Deleting transcript rows, description rows, or mixed selections no longer fails because another saved transcript row overlaps. Source video/audio, video duration, and all remaining timestamps stay unchanged; description audits refresh and removed description narration is cleaned up normally.

Read-only diagnosis of the screenshot's project (`YouTube-ebYDpoKxmEA-47df91f8`): duration is 714 seconds (11:54); all transcript and description ranges are within it. The earlier 52–103 second transcript overlaps 66–81 and 97–109 second rows. The old whole-transcript validator incorrectly blocked deletion of the unrelated 124–149 second song and also blocked description deletion. Reproduced the old error and verified fixed transcript-only, description-only, and mixed saves using in-memory copies of this manifest. The user's actual project was not changed or its song removed.

## 2026-10-31 — Hide API-key deletion when empty (1.0.193 / build 193)

Mac and Windows now hide the Delete Gemini API key control for empty or whitespace-only input, rather than leaving a disabled trash icon visible. It appears only when a key is present, updates as the field changes, and disappears after deletion. Both delete actions reject an empty key before confirmation or removal; Windows rechecks after confirmation, and the Mac model rejects empty/busy deletion requests. The Mac secure field uses observable local input state so visibility responds immediately while credentials remain stored only in Keychain.

## 2026-10-27 — Single installed app copy workflow (build tooling) — version 1.0.148 (build 148)

`script/build_and_run.sh` now builds into `build/DerivedData.noindex` (the `.noindex` suffix keeps Spotlight from indexing build output) and, for every mode except `--build-only`, replaces `/Applications/VidiStudio.app` with the newly signed bundle, removes the DerivedData app, and unregisters it from LaunchServices before launching the installed app. Normal and verification runs build Release; `--debug` builds Debug; `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIGURATION` overrides both. Installation stages the new bundle and verifies its signature before deleting the previous copy, so a failed update cannot destroy the working install. `--build-only` keeps the signed product for `script/package.sh` (path updated) but unregisters it. This keeps Spotlight, LaunchServices, Open With, and app pickers down to one VidiStudio entry after rebuilds. Documented the required single-installed-copy policy in the workspace, Mac, and Windows `AGENTS.md` files and updated the Mac README; the Windows rule requires deleting prior registered packages and stale unpacked `bin` copies before registering or installing a new build. All three guides now also state that every version update must install the new build over the previous copy on the host (Mac: `/Applications`, Windows: remove the old package then register/install) before the bump counts as complete. No app source, menu, or version change.

Validation: `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` rebuilt Release into the new DerivedData path (menu parity and 13-page Help checks passed), installed 1.0.148 over the previous copy, and launched `/Applications/VidiStudio.app`; `mdfind -name VidiStudio.app` and the LaunchServices dump listed only `/Applications/VidiStudio.app`; the installed bundle is 1.0.148 with a valid deep signature, no build product remained, and both Project-menu items were present. `--build-only` kept the signed product for packaging, left Spotlight with only the installed app, and removed its LaunchServices entry; the leftover Debug test product was deleted afterward. Not run: Windows registration/cleanup (no Windows host), `script/package.sh` artifact creation, or live Gemini.

## 2026-10-25 — Project file integrity and damaged-project reporting — version 1.0.122 (build 122)

VidiStudio now audits every project's on-disk files when it loads, when the app becomes active, and every four seconds while a project editor is open. The audit separates required files (project.json and the local source video), regenerable generated content (metadata/probe.json, timeline pictures, segment narration audio, and exports), and missing folders. Recoverable deletions show an orange banner at the top of the editor with the exact missing paths, their impact, and actions to regenerate missing narration, regenerate video details and timeline pictures, export again, or reveal the folder. One regenerable category (for example every narration MP3) stays a warning even when the entire category is gone, so narration can be recreated without marking the project damaged. Missing project.json, a missing local source video, or loss of at least 75% of tracked files across multiple generated categories marks the project **Damaged**; the editor shows a red warning with a **Delete Project…** action, and All Projects shows Damaged or Files Missing badges in Grid View, List View, and accessibility descriptions. A deleted project folder produces a missing-folder error and a remove action. Folders that still contain project artifacts but no readable project.json appear as in-memory damaged placeholders so the user can delete them; restoring project.json upgrades the entry automatically. New sources are `ProjectIntegrity.swift`, `StudioProjectIntegrity.swift`, and `ProjectIntegrityView.swift`; `script/test_project_integrity.py` runs `tests/project_integrity_harness.swift` over healthy, missing-narration, missing-source, corrupt-manifest, significant-loss, YouTube, missing-folder, and path-traversal cases. Help → Projects and Troubleshooting describe the warning levels and recovery actions.

Validation: macOS `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` built, signed, and launched 1.0.122 with 13 Help pages verified and menu parity passing; the macOS suite passed 151 tests; the Windows Python suite passed 199 tests (1 Windows-only skip); both `test_menu_parity` suites passed; the new integrity harness passed. ## 2026-10-24 — Piper summaries, live timing, and voice management — version 1.0.114 (build 114)

**Summary read-aloud no longer fails on symbol-only lines.** A summary line such as a horizontal rule or bullet produced zero audio samples; Piper never set a WAV channel count, `wave` closed with `# channels not specified`, and the reader showed that raw error. The reader now skips fragments with no letters or digits (whole sentences, read-from-here partials, and one-off selections), and `piper_speech` writes a default WAV header before synthesis and then reports "This text has no words to read aloud." instead of a low-level error. `finish_system_speech` also reports a clean message when no audio stream was produced.

**Piper live preview starts on time.** The playback timer now renders the next three cues within 20 seconds before they start, one render after another, through the existing `PiperSpeech` cache. A newly started cue plays from its first word even when the render finished a moment late; seeking mid-cue still aligns to the current position. Prefetch state is cleared when narration stops.

**Windows summary playback speaks Piper and SAPI 5 directly.** `SummaryReadback` previously ran `preview-tts` in a second engine process; a Piper failure there surfaced only as "Processing could not finish." Piper and SAPI 5 sentences now synthesize through the same speech host as live narration, so failures carry their own message, and Piper-specific guidance is mapped through `ProcessingException`.

**Voice management.** The Narration settings page no longer repeats the long Piper descriptions on either desktop. Both pages now show Download or Remove Voices next to a new Delete Voices action; the delete dialog lists only downloaded voices with Play Sample, the same search, language/quality filters, and four sort orders as the download catalog, and a red Delete per row. Escape closes both the download and delete dialogs on macOS (`onExitCommand`) and Windows (`VirtualKey.Escape`).

Validation: `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` built, signed, and launched 1.0.114 with 13 Help pages verified; the packaged engine now answers punctuation-only text with "This text has no words to read aloud." and still synthesizes normal Piper speech with `en_US-ryan-high`; the macOS suite passed 150 tests; the Windows suite passed 198 tests (1 Windows-only skip); the menu parity checker and both `test_menu_parity` suites passed. ## 2026-10-24 — Piper summary read-aloud fixed — version 1.0.113 (build 113)

Summary Play with a Piper voice failed inside the app even though the same engine binary worked outside the bundle. Root cause: espeak-ng stores its data folder in a 160-byte buffer, and the packaged path (`…/VidiStudio.app/Contents/Resources/Engine/_internal/piper/espeak-ng-data`, 169 characters) silently overflowed, so espeak fell back to the builder's `/Users/runner/work/…` data path and Piper aborted with `Error processing file …/phontab` before writing audio. The engine now resolves espeak-ng data beside the frozen executable and, when that path is too long, mirrors it once into `~/Library/Application Support/VidiStudio/piper/espeak-ng-data` (marker file keyed to the bundled `phontab`; reused while the bundle is unchanged) so espeak receives a short path. Summary read-aloud also identifies the engine it uses — the reader now shows **Piper Speech** instead of a hardcoded **Apple Speech** when Piper is selected — and Piper failures show their own actionable text instead of the Apple-only message; the generic speech message now names both Apple and Piper voices. Help pages and README describe live preview and summary read-aloud with the selected Apple or Piper voice.

Validation: `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` built, signed, and launched 1.0.113 with 13 Help pages verified; the packaged app engine synthesized WAVs with `en_US-ryan-high` through `synthesize-speech` both with the relocated espeak data already present and after deleting it (fresh 19 MB copy recreated in under a second); the macOS suite passed 149 tests; the Windows suite passed 197 tests (1 Windows-only skip); the menu parity checker and both `test_menu_parity` suites passed. Interactive clicking of Summary Play inside the running app was not automated on this host.

## 2026-10-19 — Stop the recurring macOS keychain access prompt — version 1.0.98 (build 98)

Fixed the "VidiStudio wants to use your confidential information stored in 'VidiStudio Gemini API Key' in your keychain" prompt that appeared on every launch. macOS's file-based keychain attaches an access control list to an item that is matched by the code signature (designated requirement) of the process that created it, so an item created by anything other than the current signed build — an earlier differently-signed build or an external tool — prompts every time even after "Always Allow". `KeychainStore.saveToKeychain` now treats a failed `SecItemUpdate` as a foreign/stale item and replaces it (`SecItemDelete` then `SecItemAdd`) so the running build always ends up owning the item, after which macOS stops prompting. The stale item left in the login keychain was removed so the app recreates it cleanly on the next key entry; the key must be entered once more.

Validation: `xcrun swiftc -typecheck` and `-parse` passed for `KeychainStore.swift`; the macOS parity suite passed (6 tests); version synchronized at 1.0.98 (Mac build 98 / Windows package 1.0.98.0). The keychain prompt could not be reproduced on this host without the GUI session; the fix is based on the documented ACL/designated-requirement behavior.

## 2026-10-19 — Transient invocation configuration for engine API key handoff — version 1.0.92 (build 92)

Fixed the root cause of "The API key is invalid or was rejected by Google" on macOS and Windows following the move to OS credential storage. In the initial migration, `StudioSettings.apiKey` was made a computed property, which properly prevented the key from ever being persisted to `~/Library/Application Support/VidiStudio/config.json`. However, when temporary execution configs were generated for the engine in `loadGeminiModels()`, `GeminiVoicePreview.play()`, and `StudioModel.execute()`, serializing `settings` with `JSONEncoder` similarly omitted `apiKey` from the file passed via `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIG`. Frozen PyInstaller binaries (which read the `config.json` file rather than expecting the environment variable) received an invocation config with no key, reporting failure which `StudioModel` mapped to an invalid API key.

The engine handoff now operates via dual-channel transient execution:
- At rest on disk: `~/Library/Application Support/VidiStudio/config.json` NEVER contains `apiKey`. The API key is stored exclusively in the macOS Keychain (`com.vidistudio.gemini-api`).
- During engine execution: When launching the engine, `StudioModel.execute`, `loadGeminiModels`, and `GeminiVoicePreview.play` create a temporary, short-lived execution configuration with `0o600` permissions containing the Keychain-held API key, pass it to `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIG`, pass `VIDISTUDIO_API_KEY` in the environment, and delete the temporary directory immediately upon process termination.
- Tested and verified: live Gemini check against Google succeeded returning 34 models and plan tier; Keychain storage confirmed via `security find-generic-password`; app build and launch verified.

Validation: macOS packaged build passed (`script/build_and_run.sh --build-only` reported `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, bundled 104 media binaries/libraries, verified all 13 Help pages, codesigned and verified the app bundle); full macOS Python discovery in `VidiStudio Mac/tests` passed 141 tests; menu parity checkers passed on both platforms; version synchronized at 1.0.92 (Mac build 92 / Windows package 1.0.92.0).

## 2026-10-19 — Gemini API key moved to the macOS Keychain — version 1.0.91 (build 91)

The Gemini API key is no longer written to `~/Library/Application Support/VidiStudio/config.json`. It is now owned by the macOS Keychain (`VidiStudio/KeychainStore.swift`, a generic-password item with service `com.vidistudio.gemini-api`), so it is protected by the system and the user's login keychain rather than the app bundle or a plaintext config, and it survives app updates and reinstalls. `StudioSettings.apiKey` is now a computed property backed by the Keychain, so it is automatically excluded from `Codable` and can never serialize into settings; launch migrates any plaintext key left in an existing `config.json` into the Keychain and re-saves the file without it. The engine still runs every Gemini call, but now receives the key through the `VIDISTUDIO_API_KEY` process environment (`StudioModel.execute` and `GeminiVoicePreview.play`) instead of reading it from disk; the engine overlays that variable in `config()` for both this checkout and Windows. The Gemini Settings caption and the Privacy and Settings help pages now state that the key is stored in the Keychain.

Validation: macOS packaged build passed (`script/build_and_run.sh --build-only` reported `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, bundled 104 media binaries/libraries, verified all 13 Help pages, and codesigned/verified the app bundle); full macOS Python discovery in `VidiStudio Mac/tests` passed 141 tests; the desktop menu parity checker passed; versions are synchronized at 1.0.91 (Mac build 91 / Windows package 1.0.91.0). Windows native compilation/UI smoke and live Gemini requests were not run on this macOS host. The Keychain item is created under the app's stable Apple Development signing identity, so it survives routine updates and reinstalls that keep that identity.

Switching an existing Gemini selection to preview switches to the native engine, without clearing the saved Gemini voice preference or changing existing project audio. Mac Settings and AD Overview use the same eligibility rule; settings property changes, startup decoding, and external settings updates enforce it. Windows refreshes engine/voice choices immediately, stops any active sample when playback mode changes, and normalizes invalid stored combinations at load/reload/save and programmatic changes (including the Mac `device` alias).

## 2026-10-18 — Unified AD audio generation controls and primary-voice routing — version 1.0.80 (build 81)

Reduced AD Overview, Inspector, and Project → Narration → Narration Audio to the same two bulk audio commands on both desktops. The primary button is **Generate All AD Audio** before any segment has recorded audio and **Regenerate All AD Audio…** afterward; there is no separate Synthesize or Regenerate All Narration button. **Generate Missing or Changed AD Audio** remains visible but disabled until audio exists, and is then enabled only for missing/failed/text/timing repairs. It handles partial projects as well as edited ones; current audio is preserved. Removed the redundant Windows Inspector single-segment generation button (segment context actions remain).

Root cause of continued stale primary-voice warnings: AD Overview's old Generate All button bypassed the explicit full-regeneration flag and retained usable audio plus the previous primary voice in partially narrated projects. Every unqualified whole-project engine generation is now a full run that attempts every segment and saves the selected primary engine/voice before generation. Mac and Windows UI dispatchers also normalize all-project requests to this mode, sharing the same replacement confirmation only when audio already exists. Repair-only and individual generation remain explicit separate modes and preserve the baseline; genuine failed replacements retain truthful old-file metadata.

## 2026-10-17 — Resilient narration generation with a per-segment failure table and changed-only enablement — version 1.0.65 (build 66)

Narration generation no longer stops at the first description that cannot be synthesized. `synthesize` in `engine/vidistudio_engine.py` now catches per-description failures, deletes the partial output file, records the failure (index, start, end, duration, reason), emits a warning-level line for that description, and continues with the remaining descriptions. Cancellation still stops the run immediately because `ProcessingCancelled` is re-raised. The run ends with a summary event that carries `narrationFailures` (`[{index,start,end,duration,reason}]`) and `narrationGenerated`.

The macOS client parses `narrationFailures` in `StudioModel` and presents a `NarrationFailuresView` sheet that lists Start, End, Duration, and Reason for every skipped description next to the count that succeeded, so a single bad segment is actionable instead of fatal.

"Synthesize Changed Segments" is now enabled only when at least one description changed after its narration was generated. A new `EditorShortcutActions.hasChangedNarration` flag (set from `SynchronizedAudioStatus.resolve(cue:project:) == .needsRegeneration`) gates the menu item (`KeyboardShortcuts.swift`), the editor panel button (`ContentView.swift`), and the run action. The AD Overview "Generate Missing or Changed Audio" button is enabled only while audio is missing or stale (`ADOverviewView.swift`).

## 2026-10-17 — In-project progress indicators for Transcript, Summary, and Descriptions — version 1.0.63 (build 64)

Analysis no longer replaces the editor content with a blocking card. Each section now shows its own progress in place: an inline spinner with a stage title (Generating transcript / Generating summary / Generating audio descriptions, or Preparing to upload / Uploading video) appears over the section being generated, a "Waiting to generate …" spinner appears over sections still in the queued plan, and a small ring also sits beside the section group in the inspector. A slim status bar above the section content carries the same stage title, the live engine message, and a Cancel button, and it appears only for section analysis. Navigation between sections stays enabled during an analysis run; the whole workspace is no longer locked.

The plan is derived from the command (`analyze`/`regenerate-all` = transcript → summary → descriptions, plus the single-stage transcribe, summary, and description commands) and trimmed by `--start-stage`. Because the engine emits `processingSection` only when a stage starts and `videoPreparation` while the Files API upload runs, the model keeps a queued plan and advances on each stage-start event, clearing everything on completion, failure, or cancellation.

## 2026-10-17 — High-contrast red playhead and smooth playback synchronization across timeline tracks — version 1.0.62 (build 63)

Implemented a high-contrast red playhead across all timeline tracks and resolved video playback synchronization lag:
- High-visibility red playhead system: Replaced the previous 2px blue `.tint` playhead lines with a high-contrast red (`#E52B32`) playhead across all 140px of timeline tracks (Thumbnails, Transport, Transcript, and Audio Description).
  - Thumbnail track: 12px circular handle with white fill, 2.5px red stroke, and 4px red center dot, paired with a 3px bold red needle featuring a subtle drop shadow and 0.5px white outline for sharp visibility across light and dark video frames.
  - Transport track: 1.5px semi-transparent red hairline guide connecting the top and bottom sections.
  - Transcript & Audio Description tracks: Red downward-pointing triangle pointer (`DownwardTriangle`, 14×8) with a 3px bold red needle with shadow and white outline extending across both tracks.
- Full playback synchronization & smooth performance:
  - Synchronous main-actor AVPlayer observer: Reduced the `CMTime` periodic observer interval from 0.15s (150ms) to 0.033s (~30 fps) and replaced asynchronous `Task { @MainActor in ... }` hopping with synchronous `MainActor.assumeIsolated { ... }` execution, eliminating playback stutter, timeline lag, and rubber-banding.
  - Active drag/scrub protection: Added `@MainActor var isScrubbing = false` state to `PlaybackModel` and updated `seekSurface` with continuous `DragGesture` tracking. AVPlayer time updates are guarded so playback cannot fight or overwrite user-controlled scrub positions.
  - Exact frame seek tolerance: Configured player seek operations with `.zero` tolerance (`toleranceBefore: .zero, toleranceAfter: .zero`) for exact frame positioning.
  - YouTube playback synchronization: Tuned the YouTube iframe bridge polling interval from 150ms to 40ms, respecting scrubbing state.
  - Universal timeline scrub surface: Added seek surface drag support across both the thumbnail strip and segment tracks (Transcript and Audio Description).
- Version bump: Advanced app and Share extension Debug and Release settings from 1.0.61 (build 62) to 1.0.62 (build 63).

Validation: Required `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIGURATION=Release ./script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed with macOS access, including 13 packaged Help pages, registration, anchors, links, style, search index, deep signing, and launch (process 47139). Release bundle metadata reports 1.0.62 / build 63. All 110 regression tests passed in 46.965 seconds with `VIDISTUDIO_TEST_ENGINE` pointing to the verified Release executable at `build/DerivedData/Build/Products/Release/VidiStudio.app/Contents/Resources/Engine/vidistudio-engine`. `git diff --check` passed.

## 2026-10-17 — Invalid Gemini API key detection, feature gating, and error reporting — version 1.0.61 (build 62)

Comprehensive handling and user-facing safeguards for invalid, expired, or malformed Gemini API keys across the application:
- Clean Engine Error Reporting: Enhanced `safe_error()` in `engine/vidistudio_engine.py` to recognize `API_KEY_INVALID` and HTTP 400 client errors from the Gemini API and format them into clean, human-readable errors (`"API key not valid. Please check your Gemini API key in Settings."`) rather than exposing raw JSON error payloads or stack dumps, while maintaining strict redaction of keys.
- Engine Command Error Handling: In `list_models()`, `check-api-key`, and all Gemini-invoking operations, uncaught exceptions from invalid keys now cleanly emit the sanitized error message and exit with non-zero exit codes. Added unit tests in `tests/test_voice_preview.py` validating sanitized error formatting and model listing behavior under invalid API keys.
- User-Facing Issue Mapping: Updated `UserFacingIssue.forEngine()` in `UserFacingIssue.swift` to specifically detect invalid API key responses and present actionable dialog titles and explanations: `"Invalid Gemini API Key: Your Gemini API key could not be verified by Google AI Studio. Check and update your API key in Settings → Gemini API."`
- Model State & Feature Gating: In `StudioModel.swift`, added `hasValidApiKey` (`hasApiKey && apiKeyVerified != false`) and `isApiKeyInvalid` (`hasApiKey && apiKeyVerified == false`). When key check fails or an API call rejects the key, `apiKeyVerified` is set to `false`, `availableGeminiModels` is cleared, and `geminiModelsLoaded` is reset to `false`.
- Global UI Feature Disabling & Tooltips:
  - Project Browser: When an API key is invalid, displays a prominent red warning banner (`"Invalid Gemini API Key: The entered Gemini API key could not be verified. AI features are disabled across VidiStudio until a valid key is entered."`) with an "Open Gemini API Settings…" shortcut button. Analysis actions (Analyze Video, Generate Transcript, Generate Summary, Generate Audio Descriptions) in the Project Browser and context menus are disabled with clear tooltips explaining that the key is invalid and must be updated.
  - Project Editor: Displays the red "Invalid Gemini API Key" banner across the editor header; generation buttons, Gemini TTS narration buttons, video preparation / Files API buttons, frame text OCR, and segment re-analysis buttons are disabled with explanatory tooltips.
  - New Project Dialog: YouTube Fetch Details and Create Project buttons are disabled when the key is invalid, accompanied by inline red diagnostic warnings.
  - Keyboard Shortcuts: Gated `EditorShortcutActions.canRun()` with `hasValidApiKey` so shortcuts for AI operations (`transcribe`, `generate-summary`, `generate-ad-cues`, `reanalyze-selection`, and Gemini TTS) cannot be triggered with an invalid key.
- Settings Presentation:
  - Under "Connect to Gemini", when verification fails, a red warning card is displayed with icon `xmark.octagon.fill`, title `"API Key Check Failed (Invalid Key)"`, the exact diagnostic message, and a clear notification that all AI features remain disabled until a valid key is provided.
  - In the "Account Plan" section, an invalid key now displays a red `"Invalid Key"` status capsule with the explanation `"The plan tier cannot be determined because the API key is invalid"`, replacing the previous fallback to "Free Plan".
- Startup Verification: Background key check is automatically initiated when `ContentView` appears if an API key is present and unverified (`apiKeyVerified == nil`), immediately detecting invalid keys on application launch.
- Version bump: Advanced app and Share extension Debug and Release settings from 1.0.60 (build 61) to 1.0.61 (build 62).

## 2026-10-17 — Automatic API plan detection and simplified settings display — version 1.0.60 (build 61)

Simplified the Gemini API settings workflow and automated Free vs. Paid plan detection:
- Automatic API Plan Detection: The application engine now inspects the Gemini API dynamically via `detect_gemini_plan` during model listing and key verification. It checks access to paid-tier-only models (such as `gemini-omni-1.1-flash`) and context caching capabilities (`client.caches.list()`) to automatically determine whether the API key is on the Free Plan or Paid Plan, automatically selecting and persisting the plan in user config without requiring manual selection.
- Automatic verification on key entry: The API key SecureField in Settings → Gemini API now triggers automatic verification and plan detection upon entry (via submit or debounced typing) and on view appearance, updating verification status and model availability seamlessly.
- Simplified Settings UI: Removed the manual Plan Tier segmented picker and the detailed Functionality & Plan Limits breakdown containing Google AI Studio quota tables. The settings page now simply displays whether the entered API key is on the Free Plan or Paid Plan with a clean status badge and concise description.
- Documentation and Help: Updated `docs/help/settings.html` to document automatic plan detection and rebuilt the 13-page Apple Help book bundle.

## 2026-10-17 — Gemini API key enforcement and Plan capabilities breakdown — version 1.0.59 (build 60)

Implemented system-wide API key enforcement, feature gating, live API key check, and Gemini plan capability breakdown across the Mac application:
- API key requirement & feature gating: When no Gemini API key is configured, all AI-dependent buttons throughout the app are disabled and display prominent, clear explanatory messages and tooltips directing users to Settings → Gemini API.
  - Project Browser: Top warning banner appears when API key is missing with direct button to open Settings; Analysis menu actions (Analyze Video, Generate Transcript, Generate Summary, Generate Audio Descriptions) are disabled with informative tooltips.
  - New Project Sheet: Fetch Details and Create Project for YouTube sources are disabled when missing an API key with inline warning banner.
  - Project Editor: Prominent top warning banner alerts the user when an API key is missing with a shortcut button to Settings; toolbar buttons (Read Frame Text, Generate Audio Descriptions/Analysis), segment actions (Rewrite to Fit, Synthesize This Segment, Re-Analyze This Segment), SegmentEditor actions, and ProcessingInspector actions are gated and explain the missing key.
  - Audio Description & Voice Previews: Generate All AD Audio and Generate Missing or Changed Audio are disabled when using Gemini TTS without an API key; voice preview sample generation is disabled with inline warning notices.
  - Keyboard Shortcuts: Editor shortcuts for AI operations (`transcribe`, `generate-summary`, `generate-ad-cues`, `reanalyze-selection`, and Gemini TTS synthesis) are gated in `canRun(_:)` to prevent trigger without an API key.
- Check API Key & Plan: Added "Check API Key & Plan" button to Settings → Gemini API that validates the configured key against the Gemini API catalog via a new `check-api-key` engine command, displaying live verification status badges (Checking, Verified, Failed) and diagnostic messages.
- Free vs. Paid Plan breakdown: Added an Account Plan segmented selector (Free Plan vs. Paid Plan) in Settings → Gemini API alongside an interactive `GeminiPlanCapabilitiesMatrixView` detailing rate limits, supported workflows, and capabilities for Free vs. Paid tiers across 8 distinct capabilities (Speech & Audio Transcription, Video Summaries, Audio Descriptions, On-Screen Text OCR, Description Rewrites, Gemini TTS Narration, YouTube Video Analysis, and Data Privacy & Training Terms).
- Documentation and Help: Updated `docs/help/settings.html` with API key requirement, key verification, plan comparison, and capabilities reference; rebuilt the 13-page Apple Help book bundle.
- Version bump: Advanced app and Share extension Debug and Release settings from 1.0.58 (build 59) to 1.0.59 (build 60).

## 2026-10-04 — Standard video transport with Play/Pause and Stop — version 1.0.56 (build 57)

Replaced separate Play and Pause buttons beneath the timeline thumbnails with one state-dependent Play/Pause button, alongside dedicated Stop, five-second Rewind, and five-second Fast Forward controls. A small shared VideoPlaybackControls SwiftUI view gives local and YouTube videos the same centered row, regular native borderless buttons, consistent 16-point SF Symbols, and equally sized control content. Five-second seeks use gobackward.5/goforward.5; playback uses play.fill/pause.fill and stop.fill. Accessible labels and tooltips follow the current action. Stop Video is available from Playback and Command-Shift-Period; the existing Command-Shift-Space and seek shortcuts are retained. README, Editor Help, Settings shortcut reference, and generated keyboard Help describe the behavior.

Pause retains the current position. Stop pauses video and synchronized narration, restores soundtrack volume, and seeks to the beginning while retaining the loaded player, full-screen surface, and timeline observer. Local pending seek/play completions are cancelled and guarded by request identity so Stop or Pause cannot be undone by a queued start. YouTube Stop pauses before and after seeking to zero, including from ended/cued states. Buffering keeps the Pause action available but remains separate from actual playing state so narration does not start during buffering. ## 2026-10-03 — Automatic YouTube audio download retries — version 1.0.54 (build 55)

YouTube Audio Only and YouTube Audio with Narration now retry a failed complete soundtrack download up to two more times. Network/downloader failures and missing or empty completed files trigger fresh extraction and download attempts after 2-second and 4-second waits. Each attempt uses a fresh downloader and an isolated temporary directory; failed partial or stale completed files are discarded before retrying. The shared export status displays the next attempt number, and Cancel Export remains available during the wait. Existing request/fragment retries, the overall 900-second worker deadline, process-group cancellation, original-track preference, public-video checks, file-size limits, and sanitized errors remain in place. ## 2026-10-03 — AAC video soundtracks and transcript-track audit — version 1.0.53 (build 54)

Audited all video containers and normal/descriptive transcript exports. A PCM-source fixture proved that MKV, MOV, MP4, and M4V previously kept non-AAC original audio when the muxer accepted it. Video exports now preserve existing AAC source audio and convert other source codecs to AAC; original and described tracks receive explicit titles, language metadata, default ordering, and accessibility dispositions. Open-described video now labels its single audio track as described. Descriptive caption tracks carry the Matroska text-description flag or MP4 description role. HLS no longer falsely labels ordinary transcripts as music-and-sound/SDH captions. It declares stereo AAC channels, retains distinct normal/descriptive caption names, and avoids identical auto-selection characteristics by making descriptive transcripts manually selectable. Standalone SRT/VTT retain saved text and timing; embedded descriptive captions retain their existing Audio description prefix. ## 2026-10-03 — Video and audio Format pickers — version 1.0.48 (build 49)

Added a native Format menu to the shared Video and Audio export panels. Video offers MKV (the default), MP4, MOV, and M4V. Audio offers exactly WAV (the default), MP3, and M4A for every local and YouTube audio choice. Audio option names now identify content without format suffixes. YouTube choices retain their requested order: YouTube Audio Only, Narration Audio Only, YouTube Audio with Narration. Format selection travels with the export action, controls the engine's container/codec and filename, and participates in replacement confirmation. Different formats coexist in Created Exports; MKV is recognized as a video throughout export groups and content status. Export explanations remain removed.

The engine accepts --media-format and validates it against the selected category before rendering. WAV is uncompressed 24-bit PCM with automatic RF64 for large output; MP3 is 320 kbps; M4A is 256 kbps AAC. MKV preserves source video for closed captions, uses selectable SubRip tracks with transcript titles/language metadata, and carries the standardized visual-impaired flag on the described audio track. MP4/MOV/M4V use timed-text captions. MOV continues to receive the native Apple accessibility finalizer; other containers complete through engine validation without entering the MOV-only finalizer. Existing command-line defaults stay compatible when --media-format is omitted; the dialog explicitly supplies MKV/WAV. ## 2026-09-23 — Source-resolution HLS ladder with 4K, 1080p, 720p, and 480p — version 1.0.41 (build 42)

Changed Web Playback so its highest HLS rendition preserves the local source video's complete even-pixel resolution instead of capping every export at 720p. VidiStudio adds each smaller standard rendition that fits without upscaling: a 4K source produces 4K, 1080p, 720p, and 480p; a 1080p source produces 1080p, 720p, and 480p; a 720p source produces 720p and 480p; and nonstandard or larger-than-4K sources keep an exact source-resolution rendition before the applicable standard tiers. Videos smaller than 480p retain their source resolution and the existing high/economy bitrate choices rather than being enlarged.

Each rendition now uses resolution-appropriate H.264 level declarations, codec identifiers, bitrate ceilings, and master-playlist bandwidth values. The 4K tier uses High Profile level 5.2 metadata, the 1080p tier uses level 4.2, and lower tiers retain the established compatible level. HDR/HLG sources still keep their source resolution while being tone-mapped to BT.709 SDR for browser compatibility. Progress messages now say 4K, 1080p, 720p, and 480p as those renditions are created. The Export dialog's plain-language description, open-caption warning, README, and bundled Export Help now explain that source resolution is preserved, smaller tiers are added, and smaller sources are never upscaled.

Added rendition-selection regression coverage for 1080p, 4K, and larger-than-4K sources, including codec declarations, plus a real-media 4K HLS regression that checks all four generated playlist resolutions and the master manifest. App and Share extension Debug/Release settings advanced together from 1.0.40/build 41 to 1.0.41/build 42.

Validation: the focused rendition-selection and HDR tone-map regressions passed, and a real 1.5-second 3840×2160 media export produced independently probeable 4K, 1080p, 720p, and 480p HLS video playlists plus a four-stream master playlist with the expected 4K codec declaration. All 87 tests then passed in 22.324 seconds against the exact Release-packaged engine with real FFmpeg media processing and macOS system speech. `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIGURATION=Release ./script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed, including packaged registration, all 13 Help pages, topic anchors, links, styling, search index, signing, launch, and process 7926. The built app reports version 1.0.41/build 42, and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passed. ## 2026-09-14 — Finder Share launch repair — version 1.0.5 (build 6)

Fixed the Finder Share failure that displayed “macOS could not open VidiStudio.” The Share extension had used `NSExtensionContext.open`, but Finder immediately completed that request with `false`; macOS Share extensions cannot use that host-context route to open their containing app. The extension now derives and validates its embedded containing `.app` URL, then uses `NSWorkspace` to open the typed `vidistudio://share` payload explicitly with that exact VidiStudio bundle. It activates the existing instance when available, does not add the handoff to Recents, completes the Share request only after Launch Services reports success, and surfaces the underlying launch error if one occurs. The extension remains sandboxed with read-only access to the user-selected file, while all project writes remain in the containing app and packaged engine.

Following the repository version policy, Debug and Release now use `MARKETING_VERSION` 1.0.5 and `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` 6 for both the app and extension. The final `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIGURATION=Release ./script/build_and_run.sh --verify` succeeded, passed all 13 packaged Help checks, signed the nested extension, passed strict deep signature verification, and launched process 35663. A real Finder share of `MIME drama presentation on _Save Water Save Life_ _ _Save Water Save Life_ MIME Presentation.mp4` no longer showed the launch error: VidiStudio created project `227ddb0b-9757-41a0-a5fe-57efe14cfa3b`, copied the complete 120 MB movie into the project's `source/` folder, generated eight thumbnails, and saved the project with status `Imported`. Unified AppKit logs show the main project-browser window followed by a second SwiftUI window ordered to the front after import, which is the automatic project-editor opening path. The same movie was shared again through the final warning-clean extension build; the Share UI completed without an error and the library remained at exactly the same three project folders, exercising the reopen-existing-editor path without making a duplicate. Direct accessibility-tree inspection of the editor was attempted but remained unavailable because the computer-use service timed out while attaching to VidiStudio.

The focused Swift share-link harness passed. All 44 source-engine tests and all 44 tests against the engine embedded in the Release app passed through the repository virtual environment, including real-media import/export and macOS speech. `plutil` validation and `git diff --check` passed. The app and extension Info plists both report 1.0.5 (6), strict nested signature verification passed, the extension retains only App Sandbox and read-only user-selected-file entitlements, and `pluginkit` reports the Release extension registered and enabled at version 1.0.5.

## 2026-09-14 — Finder and browser Share extension — version 1.0.4 (build 5)

Added a native macOS Share extension named VidiStudio. Its activation rule accepts one movie or one web URL, resolves Finder file URLs and Safari/browser page URLs, rejects non-YouTube web pages, and hands the typed source to the containing app through the registered `vidistudio://share` URL. The extension presents a compact native progress/error view with accessible status announcements. It is sandboxed and has read-only user-selected-file access; the main app remains the sole owner of project writes and continues to invoke the packaged engine.

The main app now validates the shared payload, uses the existing local-video and YouTube import paths, and opens the project editor only after the engine has emitted and reloaded the completed project. Local movies therefore retain the existing copy-into-`source/` behavior and 2 GB limit. YouTube shares retain canonical URL/title/thumbnail behavior without downloading the video. Sharing a source that already has a project focuses that editor instead of making a duplicate. README and the bundled Projects/first-project Help topics document the workflow and the System Settings enablement path.

Following the repository version policy, Debug and Release now use `MARKETING_VERSION` 1.0.4 and `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` 5 for both the containing app and Share extension. `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIGURATION=Release ./script/build_and_run.sh --verify` succeeded, embedded and validated `VidiStudioShareExtension.appex`, passed all 13 packaged Help pages, and launched process 33922. Strict deep signature verification passed; the extension signature contains the App Sandbox and read-only user-selected-file entitlements. `pluginkit` reported the launched Release extension as registered at version 1.0.4. The focused Swift share-link harness passed URL round trips, special-character file paths, supported YouTube forms, and malicious/unsupported-link rejection. All 44 source-engine tests and all 44 tests against the engine embedded in the Release app passed, including the real-media copy/import pipeline and macOS speech.

Runtime evidence: System Settings' Sharing extensions sheet visibly lists VidiStudio with its switch currently off, which explains why the pre-enable Finder Share menu did not yet show it. A safe `vidistudio://share` handoff using a deliberately nonexistent MOV reached the exact Release app and visibly presented its “Video File Unavailable” validation alert; the All Projects view remained at two projects, so the check did not alter the user's library. The actual Finder/Safari extension action, successful creation in the user's configured library, source copy, and automatic editor opening await the user's post-enable acceptance test to avoid adding or deleting test projects in their real library.

Created `VidiStudio/StudioIcon.icon` in Apple's bundled Icon Composer with the original transparent 1024-pixel artwork as a foreground layer. Default appearance uses System Light and the dark specialization uses System Dark. Both previews were visually inspected in Icon Composer. Debug and Release target settings now select StudioIcon; the Release asset compiler processed the .icon document and emitted StudioIcon.icns plus the asset catalog. Release packaging, strict signature verification, and DMG checksum verification passed. Icon appearance follows macOS icon appearance preferences; the app's own theme selector does not override the Dock icon.

Implemented the native macOS 26 SwiftUI app in the existing plain-app Xcode project. The live logged-in website was reviewed read-only for the project list, video preview, timed description editor, narration status and export workflow. The website repository was not modified.

## Delivered

- Native project sidebar, video preview with Apple's AVKit controls, thumbnail and cue timeline, transcript/summary panels, description editing, processing inspector, Settings, keyboard menu shortcuts, and accessible control labels.
- System Liquid Glass sidebar/toolbars/buttons, SF Symbols, system typography, and macOS icon assets generated from the website's original 1024px PNG.
- Local project folders, atomic JSON writes, project-confined paths, OS write locks, pre-operation history snapshots and remembered project selection.
- Portable Python CLI for import, metadata, thumbnails, extraction, Gemini Files API transcription/summary/descriptions, macOS/Windows system narration, optional Gemini TTS, cue saving and final FFmpeg exports.
- Self-contained Python 3.14.6 engine with google-genai 2.22.0; bundled FFmpeg/FFprobe 9.0.1 plus 19 dependency libraries. No external non-system Mach-O dependencies found across 106 binaries in the inspected bundle.
- No Firebase, Firestore, authentication or cloud-storage runtime dependency.
- Xcode engine-bundling build phase, Codex Run action, build/run script, Windows engine packaging script, and Release ZIP/DMG packaging.

- `script/build_and_run.sh --verify`: Debug build and launch passed.
- `VIDISTUDIO_CONFIGURATION=Release script/build_and_run.sh --verify`: Release build and launch passed.
- `script/package.sh`: Release app, ZIP and DMG produced.
- Source engine tests: 4 passed. Packaged Debug engine tests: 4 passed. Packaged Release engine tests: 4 passed. Logs in `build/test-results.log`, `build/packaged-engine-tests.log`, `build/release-engine-tests.log`.

It verifies stale narration and missing API-key failures. Additional tests check timing/dialogue overlap and path traversal.
- Native UI: imported generated VidiStudio Demo video; created a timed cue; saved through the engine; generated macOS narration; exported an MP4; reopened saved project in Release. Settings and playback controls appear in the accessibility tree.
- `codesign --verify --deep --strict`: passed for final Release bundle.
- `hdiutil verify dist/VidiStudio-macOS-arm64.dmg`: passed; log in `build/dmg-verification.log`.
- Release-only SwiftUI VideoPlayer overlay crash was diagnosed from the crash report and fixed with a narrow native AVPlayerView representable. Native player view has no intrinsic video-pixel width and oversized restored windows are constrained to the screen.

The app and installers contain the Python engine and media binaries. They target Apple Silicon and macOS 26+. Signatures are ad hoc for local use. Public distribution notarization was not performed.

## Remaining validation limits

- Gemini analysis and premium TTS are implemented against the official SDK but were not live-tested: no Gemini key was entered into desktop Settings during this task. The app requires a user-supplied key/model configuration for those actions.
- Windows paths and System.Speech implementation are present; no Windows runtime test or Windows executable build was performed on this Mac.
- Accessibility labels and native keyboard controls were inspected, but a complete manual VoiceOver audit was not performed.
- This is the initial local workflow requested, not full feature parity with the web app: YouTube ingestion, paused-frame Gemini OCR, AI cue rewrite/auto-fit, undo UI, subtitle export, and synchronized AD preview are not included. Existing local cue text and timing can be edited and narration can be previewed individually; final mixed videos can be played after export.

### Video volume reduction during narration — 2026-09-08

Added soundtrack ducking for saved narration playback and live Apple Speech. While narration is playing, the native video's volume is reduced to 20% of the user's selected volume; the original volume is restored when narration ends, the cue ends, playback pauses, narration is disabled, seeking occurs, or the editor stops playback. Missing/nonplaying narration does not trigger ducking. Volume changes made during narration are retained as the new baseline, and mute state is untouched. The YouTube player uses the same relative reduction and restoration through its JavaScript player controls. Existing playback-position updates check actual narration playback/speech state, including completion before the segment end.

The editor's Back to All Projects button brings the browser forward and closes only that editor window.

### Transient narration playback and Read & Speak voice resolution — 2026-09-09

Narration during playback is now deliberately transient. Every newly opened project starts with Play synthesized narration, and Save Settings persists that default instead of remembering a project's temporary Preview segment text with Apple Speech choice. Saving Settings still broadcasts the current transient choice and all voice controls to already-open project windows, so live preview can be selected and used for the current editing session without becoming the next project's default. The Narration pane explains this behavior.

System default Apple speech now explicitly resolves the current-language voice from macOS Accessibility → Read & Speak for both live `AVSpeechSynthesizer` playback and Python-engine `/usr/bin/say` narration generation. The Narration pane displays the resolved voice name. An explicit VidiStudio voice override remains higher priority. The effective Read & Speak identifier is included in the narration-settings signature so an explicit Generate Missing or Changed Audio action can identify files created with earlier settings; the preservation rule documented below keeps those files playable and exportable until their segment text changes.

Xcode integration rebuilt and launched the Release app. On this Mac, direct preference resolution returned `com.apple.voice.enhanced.en-US.Nathan`, and the live Narration pane displayed “Selected: System default (Nathan (Enhanced)).” The system-default sample action ran without an app error. Allison Enhanced was then restored and saved; the configuration retained `systemVoice: Allison (Enhanced)` while persisting `narrationPlayback: generated`. A config containing the prior `device` choice was loaded into a newly opened editor and visibly reset to Play synthesized narration. For the current open editor, Preview segment text with Apple Speech was selected and saved, Narration was enabled at the 00:04 AD segment, and playback ducked the video from 100% to 20% while live speech was active without playing the missing-synthesized-audio path. The final Release app was restarted as process 32316 so the editor is again paused with Narration off and its playback choice reset to Play synthesized narration. No project narration was synthesized and no project content was modified during the live check.

### Preserve generated narration across voice changes — 2026-09-09

Existing WAV/MP3 narration is now considered synchronized whenever its saved text still matches the AD segment and its file exists. Its recorded voice/settings signature remains provenance and continues to control whether the explicit Generate Missing or Changed Audio action regenerates it, but changing the current Apple or Gemini voice no longer disables playback, labels the existing file as stale, triggers the missing-audio sound, or omits it from video and narration-only exports. Newly generated or explicitly regenerated narration still uses the latest saved voice, rate, pitch, and volume. Live Apple Speech continues to use those latest saved settings immediately and creates no project audio file.

AD Overview now displays the resolved Read & Speak voice in the same form as Settings, such as “System default (Nathan (Enhanced)).” It also explains that the overview shows the last saved voice because the Settings window is an editable draft until Save Settings is activated. Saved settings notifications now synchronize in both directions: Settings updates open project windows, and settings saved by an editor-side generation action update the All Projects/Settings model. Saving the system-default selection was observed updating the open project from Allison to System default without modifying narration files.

The user's current project has no generated narration files, so live old-voice file playback/export was not exercised against that project; the packaged engine regression covers the export rule without creating or replacing user project audio.

### Stateful description narration controls — 2026-09-09

Every description row now presents a stateful Play/Pause/Resume Narration control plus a separate Stop Narration control. Starting a segment replaces Play with Pause; pausing replaces it with Resume without losing the current position; Stop resets the preview and returns the primary control to Play. Only the active or paused segment has Stop enabled, starting a different row stops the previous preview, natural completion clears the active state, and unavailable synchronized audio disables Play/Pause. The Segment actions menu mirrors the same state and controls.

### Stable menu focus during playback and top-level Playback menu — 2026-09-09

Moved the editor's playback commands out of Project → Playback and into a dedicated top-level Playback menu. It contains Show or Hide Preview, Full-Screen Video, Play or Pause, five-second backward/forward seeking, and Toggle Narration with the existing keyboard shortcuts. Project now contains only Editor Sections, Segments, and Analysis & Regeneration.

The focused menu-command bridge is now one stable observable reference owned by `ProjectEditorWindow`, above the subtree that redraws for every playhead update. The editor updates that bridge only when command availability or project content changes. Previously, `ProjectEditor` constructed a new focused value, including new closures, whenever its body was invalidated by the 150-millisecond playback timer; SwiftUI could consequently rebuild an open menu and move focus into AVPlayer controls. Keeping the bridge identity stable prevents playhead ticks from replacing the focused command set.

### Duplicate source project prevention — 2026-09-10

New Project now rejects a local video when its case-insensitive filename matches the source filename of any existing local project known to the app. It rejects a YouTube video when its canonical 11-character video ID matches an existing YouTube project, including equivalent watch, short-link, Shorts, embed, live, and privacy-enhanced URL forms. The native preflight keeps the New Project sheet open, selects the existing project, and presents a “Project Already Exists” alert directing the user to continue with that project or delete it before continuing. The packaged Python engine independently scans every project in the selected library before probing, copying, fetching a title, or creating a project folder, so command-line and stale-view paths cannot bypass the rule.

The download is restricted to YouTube's thumbnail host, JPEG content, a validated 11-character video ID, a ten-second request timeout, and a 5 MB response limit. A failed thumbnail request does not prevent offline project creation; the existing remote-image and film-icon fallbacks remain available.

### Local video 2 GB creation limit — 2026-09-10

The native macOS app now accepts local videos up to and including 2,000,000,000 bytes and rejects anything larger immediately after the file chooser returns, before the selection is retained in New Project or Create Project becomes available. The reusable native import entry point repeats the preflight so menu and stale-dialog paths cannot bypass it. Oversized-file and unreadable-file alerts have specific accessible titles and actionable instructions.

The packaged Python engine independently applies the same limit before probing, copying, or creating an imported project; the legacy local-copy attachment command validates before probing or copying; local-video analysis validates again before Gemini upload; and the shared Gemini upload adapter refuses any over-limit file before making a network request. These changes are only in VidiStudio Mac and its packaged engine; VidiStudio Online was not modified. The Projects Help topic documents the local limit and the YouTube alternative.

Validation: three focused regressions passed for the inclusive boundary, byte 2,000,000,001, import-before-project-creation ordering, and upload-before-network ordering. The complete 36-test source suite passed 35 tests; only the existing real system-speech pipeline failed because restricted macOS speech returned no audio. The Release app and updated packaged engine rebuilt successfully with all 13 Help pages, registration, anchors, links, stylesheet, and search index verified. The packaged Release engine rejected a sparse 2,000,000,001-byte movie with the intended 2 GB message and did not create the isolated library path. An initial live New Project check exposed that URL resource metadata was unsuitable for this sparse boundary fixture, so the native policy was corrected to use the filesystem `.size` attribute; an isolated Swift execution then reported the logical byte count correctly. A post-correction live dialog pass could not be completed because the Mac locked and automatic unlock was unavailable, so the final rendered alert remains unobserved even though the corrected Swift code compiled in Release and the identical packaged boundary was exercised.

### Ephemeral YouTube thumbnail preview — 2026-09-10

Fetch Details still retrieves the YouTube name and thumbnail needed for confirmation, but now uses an explicitly ephemeral URL session with no URL cache and a reload-ignoring-cache request policy. The preview image exists only in New Project view state and is discarded when the URL changes or the sheet closes. No thumbnail file is written locally before creation. The packaged engine remains the sole owner of durable thumbnail download: `import-youtube` creates the project folders, downloads the validated JPEG into `thumbnails/youtube-thumbnail.jpg`, and records that relative path in `project.json`. The Projects Help topic now makes this lifecycle explicit.

Grid cards show the same five generated-content indicators beneath their existing duration and description count. The shared indicator model treats an existing empty transcript or AD artifact as generated, distinguishes an unavailable final-video export for URL-backed YouTube projects, and recognizes the app's MP4 and narration-audio export formats.

Every status icon has a generated, not-generated, or unavailable badge, pointer help, and a complete VoiceOver label. Table cells expose their column value directly; each grid card's combined accessibility label includes duration, transcript, summary, AD segment count, final-video exports, and narration-audio exports. Grid keeps the numeric AD count in the existing metadata line rather than repeating it in the compact icon strip. The Projects Help topic explains both layouts and the status badges.

### Automatic 3-attempt AI retry plus manual retry — 2026-09-12

Transcription, summary, and AD segment generation now retry automatically up to 3 times with exponential backoff before reporting failure. The retry wrapper lives in `engine/vidistudio_engine.py` around the single `_ai_video_once` implementation, so local videos and YouTube URL projects share the same path. Each retry emits `Retrying <stage> (attempt N of 3)` with retry metadata, and the final error reads `<Stage> failed after 3 attempt(s): <cause>. Please try again manually.` Deterministic configuration failures (missing API key/model, 2 GB limit, missing prerequisites) still fail immediately without retries, and cancellation is never retried.

The Mac UI streams engine retry messages in the existing processing status, documents the 3-attempt behavior in the Project Inspector, and every failure alert now offers a `Retry <transcription/summary/AD segments/analysis>` button alongside OK for retryable analysis commands. The failed command, arguments, and project are retained in `StudioModel` so manual retry re-runs the same request without repeating the replacement confirmation.

Validation: focused retry checks passed for 3-attempt exhaustion with the manual-retry suffix, success on the second attempt, and single-attempt configuration failure. All 25 source engine tests passed. `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed. No live Gemini validation was performed.

### Summary tab readback with synchronized highlighting — 2026-09-12

The Summary tab now has its own playback controls (Play/Pause via Command-Shift-P, Stop, Previous/Next sentence) plus a live sentence progress label. Reading uses the Apple on-device Text to Speech voice, rate, pitch, and volume from Settings → Narration through the shared `SystemSpeechUtterance` builder, speaking one summary sentence per utterance so headings, lists, quotes, and code blocks keep their existing native styling while each block's sentences are individually tracked.

The active sentence gets a full-range background fill and the spoken word gets a distinct solid background with an auto-contrasting text color, both drawn from `NSMutableAttributedString` ranges so the `AVSpeechSynthesizer` UTF-16 word ranges map exactly with wrapping intact. `Settings → Narration → Summary readback` adds Sentence highlight and Spoken word highlight color pickers with restore defaults; the sentence fill renders translucent and the word fill solid. Scrolling follows the active sentence (centered, top on start) through `ScrollViewReader`, gated by the existing Follow toggle and reduced-motion setting. Leaving the Summary tab, regenerating the summary, or closing the editor stops speech immediately so no orphan audio survives a mid-sentence close. Active sentences expose "Sentence X of N, reading/paused" VoiceOver labels with the selected trait, and controls are disabled until a real summary exists.
