### Full-screen preview restore fix — 2026-09-12

Exiting full-screen video (Escape) left the local preview collapsed, recoverable only by reopening the project. Root cause: `removeFromSuperview()` drops the superview-owned Auto Layout constraints SwiftUI laid the preview out with, and the exit path restored only the frame, which is meaningless without constraints — the next host re-layout (driven by the 150 ms playback observer) collapsed the view. The exit path now saves the surface's superview constraints on entry and re-adds them on exit before refreshing layout, with the frame restore kept as fallback. The full-screen container also exits on Escape directly as a backup to the key event monitor. A headless AppKit check confirmed the old path collapses to zero on host re-layout while the fixed path restores full size.

Validation: headless restore checks passed, Debug build succeeded, `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed with all 13 Help pages verified and the app launched, all 25 engine tests passed, and `git diff --check` passed. Live full-screen enter/exit was not separately observed in this pass.

### Bounded selection/sentence reads plus smart Play — 2026-09-12

Read Selected Text and Read This Sentence now stop automatically at the end of the selection or sentence instead of continuing like Read from Here, which still reads through to the end. Single-sentence reads go through a new `readSentence(at:)` path that keeps full sentence/word highlighting while stopping after one sentence. Play is now smart across the button, menus, and shortcut: it resumes when paused, reads from the text selection when one exists, and otherwise starts from the beginning. A headless run also exposed a stale-callback race where a late finish from stopped speech could wipe newly started reading, so delegate callbacks now verify utterance identity and rapid stop-then-start is covered by a regression check.

Validation: 10 headless checks passed covering one-shot stop, single-sentence stop, continuous advance (no regression), smart Play from selection and from start, and stale-finish immunity. Debug build succeeded, `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed with all 13 Help pages verified and the app launched, all 25 engine tests passed, and `git diff --check` passed. ### Standardized summary triggers plus deselect-on-start — 2026-09-12

Menu- and right-click-triggered reading now behaves exactly like the Play button: a headless harness driving the real model proved observation emissions fire for menu-path starts (the signal the Play/Pause label depends on), so Play/Pause/Stop reflect reading no matter which surface started it. All triggers share one `togglePlayPause()` and the same speak paths. ### Summary selection/menu delivery fix — 2026-09-12

The read-from-selection options were unreachable: the summary right-click menu never opened and the menu-bar selection items stayed disabled with text selected. A headless harness instantiating the real coordinator and text view reproduced it: AppKit posts the selection notification but never invokes the `NSTextViewDelegate` optional callbacks, so no selection ever reached the model. Selection tracking now observes `didChangeSelectionNotification` directly (the post is only a signal; the view's live range is re-read, so deferred posts can't install stale ranges) and the right-click menu is served by a `SummaryTextView.menu(for:)` subclass override with the standard menu extended in place. Programmatic restores during highlight refreshes are recorded and their echoes ignored, while any genuine user change is adopted. Item copying (instead of moving) fixed an `already is in another menu` crash with shared proposed menus.

Validation: 17 headless checks passed covering parsing, live selection reporting, both selection flags, menu contents/enabled state, ObjC action dispatch, sentence mapping, and restore-echo suppression. Debug build succeeded, `script/build_and_run.sh --verify` passed with all 13 Help pages verified and the app launched, all 25 engine tests passed, and `git diff --check` passed. Live right-click/menu-bar interaction was not separately observed in this pass.

### Streamlined analysis and industry Audio Description terminology — 2026-09-13

Simplified the visible processing workflow to one Analyze Video action plus page-specific Generate or Replace actions for Transcript, Summary, and Descriptions. The export UI, Help Book, README, engine messages, preferred CLI command names, and generated filenames now consistently use Closed Audio Description for the two-track switchable MOV and Open Audio Description for the permanently mixed MP4. The legacy `export-selectable-video` and `export-final-video` CLI command names remain accepted for compatibility.

## 2026-09-13 — Canonical project storage and automatic cleanup

Made `project.json` the single source of truth for transcript text, summary, Audio Description segments, generated-state flags, relative asset references, and the bounded segment undo/redo stacks. The engine no longer writes duplicate transcript, summary, cue, YouTube-source, OCR, whole-project history, or internal-trash JSON files. Transcription chunks, captured frames, and speech-input files are temporary; replaced narration and unlisted artifacts are removed automatically after successful operations. Empty generated folders are created only when needed, and deleting an export now permanently removes it after confirmation instead of retaining a hidden copy. A `cleanup-project` engine command migrates older folders to schema version 2 and retains only the source, probe metadata, referenced thumbnails, current narration, and listed exports.

Migrated the two projects in `~/Documents/VidiStudio Projects` after saving their original manifests in `/private/tmp/vidistudio-storage-cleanup-backup-20260913`. The local project removed 76 files and 163,188,148 bytes; the YouTube project removed 12 files and 285,163 bytes, for 163,473,311 bytes reclaimed. Canonical-content hashes matched the pre-cleanup manifests after excluding migration metadata, and every retained relative media path resolved successfully. The local project retains 47 transcript segments, a 2,454-character summary, 18 Audio Description segments with 18 narration files, eight thumbnails, six undo states, and one export. The YouTube project retains 291 transcript segments, a 3,118-character summary, six Audio Description segments, one thumbnail, and two undo states.

### Unified two-column Export dialog and combined accessible media exports — 2026-09-13

Replaced the nested Export menus in the inspector, File menu, and List/Grid project contextual menus with one native settings-style dialog. Its left sidebar selects Video, Audio, or Text, while the right pane presents that category's controls and retains fixed Cancel and Save buttons. Video requires an Audio selection (Original Audio Only, Closed Audio Description, or Open Audio Description) and a Transcript and Captions selection (none, Transcript, or AD Transcript using closed or open captions). Original Audio Only with No Transcript is rejected as a no-op. YouTube projects omit Video and keep only eligible narration/text choices.

Described video export now supports every Audio Description and caption combination in one file. Closed captions remain switchable `mov_text`; open captions use the configured dialogue/description appearance and are rendered into the picture. Closed Audio Description keeps its original/default and Apple-tagged described audio tracks when captions are added. The Audio page now also exports Original Video Audio without Audio Description and Final Audio with Audio Description as 320 kbps MP3 files, while preserving Narration Audio Only as M4A. The two MP3 choices require a local source video.

### Deterministic export replacement and per-file Finder actions — 2026-09-13

Removed date/time suffixes from newly generated export filenames. The project name and complete export configuration now identify one stable target, so repeating the same Video, Audio, or Text configuration replaces that target instead of adding another dated copy. Different audio, caption-content, caption-presentation, text-content, format, and container choices remain separate files. Existing timestamped exports remain untouched.

Replacement is explicitly guarded: the Export dialog detects the matching target and asks “Replace Existing Export?” with Cancel and Replace Export. The engine independently refuses an existing target unless it receives the confirmed replacement flag. Media and text are generated to a same-volume temporary file, validated where applicable, and atomically moved over the target, preserving the previous valid file if generation fails. The project export list keeps one entry for that configuration.

Each Created Exports row now has three accessible actions: open the export in its default app, reveal that exact file in Finder, and delete it after the existing destructive confirmation. Updated Export Help explains deterministic filenames, the replacement confirmation, legacy dated files, and all three row actions.

## 2026-09-19 — Video-preserving audio muxing and exported quality details

Changed video export to make preservation explicit and verifiable. Open or Closed Audio Description with no burned-in captions, plus all switchable closed-caption exports, now require FFmpeg video stream copy and never silently fall back to H.264 re-encoding. After muxing, the engine verifies the output codec/profile, resolution, pixel format, color space, transfer function, and color primaries against the source before accepting the export. The encoded video payload therefore remains unchanged while audio and switchable caption tracks are added; an incompatible container now causes the export to stop rather than reduce video quality.

Open captions remain the intentional exception because their pixels must be drawn into every frame. That path now keeps the source dimensions, selects high-quality H.264 or HEVC at CRF 16 based on the source, keeps supported source chroma/bit-depth formats, carries color/HDR signaling into the encoder and container, and rejects an output that changes resolution, loses reported HDR base color fields, or reduces a 10-bit source to 8-bit. Dolby Vision dynamic metadata is not claimed to survive a pixel-changing encode. Selecting open captions and choosing Save now presents a blocking warning containing the detected source resolution, codec, and color/HDR information. Use Closed Captions is the default action and changes the selection to the equivalent closed-caption choice; Export with Open Captions requires explicit confirmation.

Source Video Information now also shows measured video bit rate and Color / HDR. Each newly created or replaced video under Created Exports has an accessible Exported Video Information disclosure containing filename, whether the original stream was copied or open captions were rendered, duration, frame rate, resolution, codec/profile, video bit rate, Color / HDR, audio-track details, container, and exported size. The engine stores that measured summary portably in `project.json`; replacement refreshes it and export deletion or generated-content cleanup removes it. Legacy exports explain that one replacement is required before these measured details are available.

Advanced the app from version 1.0.10 (build 11) to version 1.0.11 (build 12), with Debug and Release settings aligned for both the app and share-extension targets.

## 2026-09-19 — People-friendly errors and video guidance

Centralized user-facing failures in `UserFacingIssue` so alerts and inline failure messages explain what happened and what the person can do next without exposing engine output, media-tool details, service responses, file-system internals, identifiers, or raw operating-system errors. Technical details are retained only in private diagnostic logging and the model's non-visual bounded log. This coverage includes project creation and storage, analysis, Gemini setup and requests, speech and narration preview, video preparation, every export path, the Share extension, and app-readiness checks.

The oversized-video alert now states the selected file size, VidiStudio's current 2 GB limit, and the available next steps; it no longer mentions the Gemini Files API. Video preparation and export progress use plain-language descriptions such as video details and timeline pictures. Open-caption guidance still clearly warns that drawing captions into the picture creates a new video and can slightly change quality or Dolby Vision information, recommends closed captions, and offers a closed-caption action by default, but it no longer exposes codec, bit-depth, FFmpeg, or re-encoding jargon in the warning. The dedicated source and exported-video information panels intentionally retain detailed quality fields because they are informational, user-requested inspection surfaces rather than error messages.

## 2026-09-20 — Targeted segment re-analysis and video availability (1.0.22, build 23)

Added Re-Analyze This Segment beside Read Frame Text in the AD segment dialog and a native Project > Segments entry/shortcut. Gemini receives the selected draft, all existing AD, full transcript, summary, legal dialogue-free ranges, and a clipped video neighborhood at 4 FPS. It proposes replacement text/timing for intersecting AD and immediate neighbors; the engine freezes unrelated segments, rejects invalid ranges/overlap, requests up to two corrections, and applies a valid result as one undoable edit. Changed narration references are invalidated while unchanged audio is retained.

Local video uploads are retained for reuse until their service-reported expiration (48-hour fallback measured from upload start), with bounded preparation and failure cleanup. Every reuse verifies actual remote status and source identity. The inspector shows human-friendly server status, last checked time, an availability countdown, Refresh Status, and a preparation control disabled for ready/processing files. It refreshes on inspector project entry and rechecks on analysis; it does not claim continuously live server monitoring. YouTube projects use direct video references.

## 2026-09-20 — Swift concurrency warning cleanup (1.0.27, build 28)

## 2026-09-20 — Targeted multilingual Transcript segments (1.0.31, build 32)

Added Transcript range generation to the shared enlarged Add/Edit Segment dialog. Start/end timing, a separate optional Gemini hint, and Re-Analyze This Segment now support one or multiple dialogue segments, including splitting/merging immediate transcript neighbors while freezing unrelated rows. Manual Save Segment remains separate. The Transcript and YouTube Transcript prompts include both shared multilingual system and user guidance, preserving original scripts and code-switching. Local range analysis uses the configured Transcript video model; when that model is audio-only, it uses the configured YouTube Transcript video model while retaining the local Transcript prompt. Existing transcript/summary generation is not a prerequisite.

Range proposals validate IDs, nonempty text, finite absolute timing, the editable neighborhood, and transcript overlaps before saving. Existing AD text, timing, narration metadata/files, and summary are preserved. Accurate dialogue that conflicts with existing AD rejects the entire proposal instead of moving/suppressing speech or altering narration. Invalid/empty proposals and network failures preserve existing content. Transcript history is separate from AD history, supports Undo/Redo through the existing menu/shortcuts and inspector, and is included in history deletion. Both history paths check restored timing against the current other track. Explicit transcript-row deletion clears transcript history to retain the existing permanent-deletion contract; full transcript regeneration and generated-content clearing also clear that history. Segment speaker metadata survives native editing. Successful edits invalidate the current export listing using the existing segment-edit behavior.

Updated Regenerate Transcript to explain full replacement, loss of manual edits/history, retained AD/narration/exports, subsequent timing review, and the Add Segment range-generation alternative. Added **Regenerate All Narration…** to the right Inspector narration controls and **Project → Narration** on both desktop platforms, sharing the guarded `regenerate-all-narration` command. ## 2026-10-29 — Existing-asset import, provenance, Markdown summary, and two-track audio — version 1.0.149 (build 149)

Implemented the complete existing-asset workflow across the shared engine and native macOS app. New Project now offers **Create Audio Description in VidiStudio** (default) or **I already have Audio Description files**, with transcript, summary, AD transcript, and AD audio pickers; AD transcript is either **AD narration only** (editable, transcript-validated) or **Dialogue + AD combined** (one caption/export track), and AD audio is **AD narration audio only** (ducked under the original) or **Full AD mix**. Actions are **Create Project & Open Editor** and **Create Project & Combine**. TXT files open a timing editor with per-line start/end fields, duration and overlap validation, **Distribute Evenly**, and optional **Auto-time with Gemini** whose proposal must be reviewed before saving. YouTube projects accept text assets only.

Added mirrored **Project → Import Existing Assets…** and an editor import button for later replacements and another combine. Import commands are atomic in the engine and copy files into the project folder (imports/, audio-description/) with portable relative paths. Every import emits an end-of-import report with imported/flagged/omitted counts, validation duration, omitted source lines and reasons, conflict ranges, warnings, and Review Descriptions/Review Transcript actions. Valid partial imports are kept; zero valid rows preserve existing content. Overlapping AD rows are imported and flagged for retiming rather than dropped; transcript edits no longer delete AD rows and instead flag them. A persistent timing banner, per-row amber **Overlap** badges, AD Overview counts, and described-export blocking make the issue visible; text exports remain available. Cleanup preserves imported assets and superseded narration is removed only after a successful commit.

The described-video model keeps **Original Audio** (default) and adds an **English - Audio Description** track with a visual-impaired disposition; **Closed Audio Description** is the default, **Open Audio Description** is an explicit single mix, and a silent source makes the described track the only/default one. The Export dialog gained an explicit **Audio Description source** picker when generated and imported audio both exist, imported source handling for closed/open video and **Final Audio with Audio Description**, and imported AD transcript caption support. combine-imported-audio uses the existing export progress/cancellation path and registers the result in project.exports.

Provenance now includes **AI-generated**, **AI-generated · modified**, **Imported**, **Imported · modified**, **Manually added**, and **Origin unknown**, with per-row and section-level labels, a persistent manual picker in Add/Edit Segment, summary-level AI/Imported/Manually added provenance, and automatic modified transitions. Per-segment **Import Narration Audio…** is in More Options and the context menu, validates decodable audio and fit (small padding tolerance, shorter allowed, longer rejected), confirms replacement, records audioOrigin/audioSourceName, and needs no API key. AD Overview gained **Narration sources**, imported-file counts, mixed-source/multiple-file warnings, imported-audio labels that are never compared as a generated voice, and legacy **Unknown voice** handling.

The Summary section now offers Generate, Import, or **Write Summary** without requiring a transcript or API key, and uses a native Markdown editor with a fixed toolbar (undo/redo, inline styles, headings 1–6, paragraph, blockquote, code block, bulleted/numbered/task lists, link, image, table, horizontal rule, clear formatting, preview, Markdown Help), selection-aware commands, one undo step per command, smart list continuation, and the documented Mac/Windows shortcut table. Markdown coverage includes tables and task lists in preview/reading; math, Mermaid, footnotes, and embedded remote images are excluded. Project List/Grid shortcuts are disabled while the summary editor is active so Command-Option-1/2 apply headings.

Validation: the shared engine and Mac suites pass 205 tests (VidiStudio Mac/.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover -s tests), including 41 import/combine tests (real FFmpeg two-track MKV metadata/defaults and open single-mix MOV) and the updated transcript/description conflict behavior. Windows Python suites pass 289 tests (1 Windows-only skip). The new native Swift harnesses pass for Markdown transformations and imported project/report decoding. Root and both desktop menu checkers pass with 39 translated shortcuts; Mac focused menu/import suites pass 27 tests and Windows focused menu/import suites pass 22 tests. python3 script/build_help.py --no-index validates all 13 Help pages; website npm run lint and npm run build pass. ## 2026-10-29 — Gemini quota/service pause and key-free project creation — version 1.0.152 (build 152)

Gemini quota, billing, and service failures now pause all AI reprocessing instead of failing one command at a time. The Mac model detects 429/RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED/quota/credit/billing/rate-limit details, 503/overloaded/service-unavailable details, and permission failures; it keeps an AIServiceIssue that makes hasValidApiKey false and disables Analyze, transcript, summary, Audio Description generation, segment re-analysis, rewrites, frame text, AI timing, and Gemini narration throughout the editor, project browser, keyboard shortcuts, export eligibility, and AD Overview. A persistent amber warning appears in All Projects, every project editor, and Settings with Retry AI and Gemini Settings actions. Retry calls the existing key/model check, and any successful AI command or key change clears the state. Invalid keys remain a separate error path, and warnings do not delete or alter existing transcripts, descriptions, narration, or exports.

New Project no longer requires a Gemini API key. The New Project shortcut/dialog opens directly instead of redirecting to Settings. Local and YouTube projects can be created without a key; when a key is configured, the engine still performs the YouTube Gemini access check, and without one it creates the project with a warning that AI analysis needs a key later. The same no-key path covers New Project from the gallery, File > New Project, share/open flows, and the Windows equivalent. Documentation and tooltips no longer describe project creation as key-gated.

## 2026-10-30 — Native sliding project panes (1.0.190 / build 190)

The Mac editor project browser now uses NavigationSplitView instead of inserting/removing an HSplitView child. A single animated binding drives the custom toolbar button, View menu, and list/grid commands; the native column visibility binding uses the same path. The default is still closed, the existing sidebar width range and list/grid controls are retained, and the automatic sidebar toggle is removed to avoid duplicating the custom control. The binding skips explicit motion when accessibilityReduceMotion is enabled.

Windows now uses native inline SplitView containers for both the left project pane and right inspector instead of immediately changing Grid column widths and Visibility. Native WinUI templates supply their directional open/close slide transitions and honor the OS animation setting. Library/menu selection guards, toolbar labels, generation fallback, and list/grid commands use IsPaneOpen as their shared target state. Closing the project pane moves keyboard focus to its toggle when focus was inside it. Native controls handle rapid reversals without custom timers. The source parity checker now verifies the native project pane starts closed and the Mac View commands use the animated binding. Both desktop menu-parity documents were updated.

## 2026-11-01 — YouTube audio sources (1.0.194 / build 194)

Added original-audio selection for YouTube projects in New Project, Import Existing Assets, individual Original Audio / AD Audio imports, and Export. Portable project fields retain the imported filename and fingerprint, independent export/playback/transcription choices, duration approval, and actual transcript/export provenance. Import accepts WAV, MP3, M4A, AAC, and FLAC; differences over 0.1 seconds or unknown video timing require explicit confirmation, with approval bound to the inspected file and video duration. Changed/missing copies require repair; regeneration and cleanup keep the imported original. Accepted differences use silence or trimming without stretching.

Unchanged transcript rows retain their data; mixed transcript sources and completed exports keep actual source labels independently of future preferences. Generated narration, imported narration, and full AD mixes share the YouTube audio export path; a full mix avoids soundtrack retrieval. Imported AD can obtain video duration through the bounded metadata-only lookup without requiring AI. Both desktop menus, help sources, native models, and versions are aligned.

## 2026-11-01 — Choose audio after YouTube export retrieval fails (1.0.196)

Added a native recovery dialog on Mac and Windows when YouTube soundtrack retrieval fails during original-audio export, described-audio export, or YouTube Import & Combine. Choose Audio File… uses the existing inspect/import path, including unreadable-file rejection, duration checking, explicit mismatch/unknown-duration confirmation, and replacement confirmation. An accepted file becomes the portable export soundtrack and retries the same command with the original format, narration source, and export-replacement choice. Playback and transcription preferences remain independent. Cancel in the failure dialog, file picker, or timing confirmation leaves the export stopped. Export cancellation and mixing/encoding failures do not offer download recovery.

The shared soundtrack resolver marks retrieval-entry failures with an explicit recovery code; exceptions after the soundtrack is yielded retain normal error handling. Native recovery waits for the failed operation to finish and clear its busy state before importing/retrying. The Windows engine client carries the recovery code through ProcessingException into the view model and owner window. Recovery retains the originating project rather than applying to an unrelated project. Canonical Help export/troubleshooting guidance, generated Windows Help, website export/recovery guide, and website FAQ now describe the flow.

Checks: Mac regression suite passes 251 tests; Windows repository suite passes 344 tests on this Mac with one platform-only skip. Five focused tests cover retrieval failure, worker-start failure, post-download mixing failure, cancellation, and imported-source bypass. A real temporary media fixture verifies that a failed download preserves a prior MP3 export, then importing a matching original completes the same MP3 replacement without another download and records the filename/source. Shared export/source parity passes (53 export/progress functions); menu parity remains aligned. The changed Windows engine client, ProcessingException, view model, audio-source controller, models, and source dialogs compile against cached WinUI/Windows SDK references with unrelated UI/speech dependencies supplied by an isolated harness. ## 2026-11-05 — Independent Gemini output languages (1.0.217 / build 217)

Settings > Gemini API now has Transcript, Summary, and Descriptions sections, each with an independent Output language selector. Auto (detect from video) is the default. Auto transcription preserves all spoken languages and original scripts; Auto summaries/descriptions use the primary language detected from the video itself, with English when no language is identifiable. A specific choice translates dialogue or generates prose in that language. The shared bundled catalogue has 110 language/script choices, including separate Simplified/Traditional Chinese, based on Google's documented Gemini text-language support checked on 2026-11-05 (https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-agent-platform/models/google-models#language_support). The Models API does not advertise per-model language lists; ASR input-language hints are not translation-output settings. Both desktop controls and engine validation use the same catalogue in ai-defaults.json. Preferences save immediately and remain independent of model choices.

Language instructions reach local/YouTube full generation, imported-audio transcription, transcript/description range re-analysis, and description rewrites. Explicit choices take precedence over original-language instructions in task and Multilingual Media prompts while retaining JSON structure, source speech timing, speaker identities, and existing validation. With a dedicated speech model selected, explicitly translated transcripts use the saved YouTube video model for local media and YouTube, including imported audio; Auto keeps the dedicated speech-model path and its automatic detection. Range editing retains its compatible-model fallback. Auto rewrites preserve the supplied description's language. OCR and TTS remain outside these three output settings. Existing content/imports are unchanged until a requested generation or range action; narration voices are selected separately. Existing English embedded-export language metadata is documented as a current limitation.

## 2026-11-05 — Language-specific saved results and section targets (1.0.221 / build 221)

Implemented portable schema 3 with stable result IDs, Auto as its own category, origin/version/timestamps, source-result and imported-file provenance, persistent review drafts, and selections remembered separately per language/content type. Existing project content migrates idempotently to Auto; selected results remain synchronized with the legacy top-level reader fields. Both desktop engines use the same language-results implementation and command dispatcher. Schema 3 projects should be used with the updated desktop versions so inactive results and their files remain protected.

Project Language is the shared default. Transcript, Summary, and Descriptions/Narration can independently choose Auto, a supported Gemini text language, or Follow Project Language. Keep All Sections in Sync resets overrides without deleting results. The inspector and matching menu/filter panels follow the active tab: transcript controls on Transcript, summary controls on Summary, description/narration controls on Descriptions/AD Overview, and the paired sources on AD Transcript. Target-language changes, project-language changes, synchronization, and Assign Language require Edit mode; assignment relabels existing content without translating it. View mode permits saved-version switching within the current language. Edit is available for empty timed sections to set their first language target. Unsaved summary drafts block language/result switching until saved or cancelled; accepting another-language review never silently changes generation targets.

Generation, imports, segment reanalysis, and narration operate on isolated alternatives. New-language results save directly; existing-language alternatives persist for Keep Both (default), Replace Selected, or Discard New review. Explicitly selected context/result IDs are captured before operations. Regenerate All runs stages without clearing other results, narration, exports, thumbnails, or history. Result-specific edits and histories remain independent. Imports label the supplied text, preserve source assets/combined AD pairing, and support narration alternatives. Batch exports freeze explicitly chosen results per language, validate selections/timing/narration before processing, retain completed outputs on failure/cancellation, and record language/result provenance with distinct names. Captions and narration tracks use language-specific titles/metadata; unknown or mixed Auto is undetermined. Native MOV accessibility finalization preserves the selected language.

Deletion selects individual results, languages, components, narration/history, optional exports, and project-wide thumbnails; exact affected result/export/file counts are previewed before confirmation. Section actions prefill the effective section language/result. Retained results, drafts, histories, and shared imported/audio files are checked before cleanup. Project browsers, saved-result review, segment filters, Created Exports, and matching menus include language/result controls; All Languages is distinct from Auto. Mac/Windows menu labels, hierarchy, guards, and stable commands match. Settings now has one Default Language for New Projects (Auto by default); a common prior three-language preference is preserved, differing preferences migrate to Auto.

## View Project Language and AD Overview — 1.0.225 / build 225

View Project Language is the single shared viewing selector on both desktops, available without Edit mode in the inspector and Language and Saved Results dialog. It switches saved transcripts, captions, summaries, descriptions, timeline, and playback together. AD Overview always follows that same selection; the additional Overview Viewing Language dropdown from interim 1.0.224 has been removed. Older overview-specific preferences are ignored by native readers and removed on engine migration. Saved-result pickers use the shared viewing language and preserve per-language choices. Independent generation targets are explicitly labelled Generation Language and remain Edit-only, as does Assign Language. Changing view does not change explicit generation targets; Follow Project Language uses the viewing language for future generation.

Top-level projections and edit histories now belong to the viewed language, while full generation can target an independent language. Segment narration, rewriting, edits, and undo/redo target the viewed result. Imports assign language metadata without changing generation targets. Regenerate All captures each stage's own target. Native Mac/Windows readers reconstruct the viewed projection directly from saved records on reopening, including provenance and histories. Engine migration adds languageViewVersion and reprojects disk-loaded manifests before capture, protecting saved Auto content from older per-section top-level projections. A language without content shows empty sections/zero overview counts. AD Overview uses the selected description result's own narration settings and timing audit; it does not inherit another language's metadata.

## Language-specific export folders — 1.0.226 / build 226

All new export types now use exports/<language code>/ inside the portable project: video, audio, subtitle/text/JSON/Markdown files, and complete Web Playback packages (for example exports/en/, exports/hi/, exports/auto/). Existing language/result identifiers in filenames are retained. Both native filename previews use the chosen export language, including when it differs from the editor viewing language. Export dialogs describe the folder layout.

The next export operation reorganizes existing tracked exports using saved exportResults language metadata; older outputs without language provenance use Auto. A complete web package moves as one directory so all player assets, subtitles, playlists, and media retain their relative links. exports, exportResults, exportMediaInfo, and exportAudioSources references are updated together; project content and internal source/narration assets are not relocated. Migration chooses numbered filenames on conflict, retains the occupied destination (including registering a previously untracked colliding export), and never overwrites it. An atomic move journal supports recovery if interrupted between renaming and manifest persistence; pending moves resume before subsequent project operations/cleanup. Move and retained-file paths are confined to the project exports tree. Repeated organization is idempotent. Invalid batch selections are validated before export-folder migration begins. Direct combined imported-audio exports now keep their new export records instead of restoring the prior export list.

Cleanup now descends through language folders, preserving ancestors of tracked files and every asset inside a retained web package while removing unreferenced temporary exports. Deleting one output/language therefore preserves retained exports in other folders. Existing replacement confirmation remains required for an output being regenerated. Failed/cancelled batches retain completed files and the repaired manifest links.

## 1.0.228 — repair historical narration references; separate audio/segment deletion (2026-11-06)

Fixed the persistent historical-narration warning after current AD audio is regenerated. After narration generation or Project Cleanup, the engine repairs dangling audio links only inside undo/redo snapshots. It shares a usable nonempty recording from a saved description result in the same language only for an exact text/start/end match. Otherwise it removes unavailable narration metadata from that older edit while preserving its text, timing and undo/redo position, so narration can be generated if restored. Each repair retains the unavailable filename, prior voice/settings and repair decision in historyNarrationRepair. Current saved-result cues and review drafts are neither replaced nor approved by this maintenance, and genuinely missing current result files still produce integrity warnings. No missing-file warning is merely hidden.

Applied this repair under the project engine lock to the user-specified Interview project. Backup: .recovery/history-narration-20261106-021329/project.json. Repaired 17 distinct unavailable historical recording references using exact same-language saved audio; one older text variant remains in undo history without a dangling audio link and needs narration if restored. Verified current saved/draft cue payloads and all 10 available recording hashes unchanged. ## 2026-11-06 — 1.0.230: automatic Windows Settings

Windows Settings now applies and persists application preferences automatically, with no Save settings or Cancel footer buttons. The native window close control/Alt-F4 retains changes. A central observer saves after a brief pause, flushes on deactivation and normal/programmatic close, retries storage failures without accepting a failed baseline, and blocks normal closure while a write is pending. Open editors receive preference updates without closing Settings, including captions, narration context, theme and Developer-mode commands. Project Settings actions continue saving to their selected project.

Only edited preferences and nested model/prompt fields are merged into the latest settings, preserving unrelated changes from other windows/engine operations. Gemini keys are passed separately to Windows Credential Manager and never included in persisted settings JSON. Explicit key deletion uses the same checked persistence path. Tests cover nested changes, deletions, external preferences, credential isolation, failed writes/retry and pending final edits. Native smoke expectations now check automatic persistence while Settings remains open, close-time flushing, editor caption refresh and the absence of footer buttons; these native checks were added but could not be executed here.

## 2026-11-06 — 1.0.233: selected multilingual video and audio tracks

Export → Video/Audio now offers One File with Selected Tracks alongside the existing separate-language export workflow. Each language has one AD Audio result picker (generated descriptions/narration or imported AD audio) and one caption/transcript result picker. AD Transcript can pair descriptions explicitly; when generated AD audio is included, its selected description result also supplies AD Transcript text. No export-all-versions choice exists. Engine validation rejects duplicate languages within either audio or caption tracks and inconsistent generated AD audio/text versions.

Selectable audio/closed captions support several languages in one container. Mixed AD audio and burned captions each allow one language across the entire file; their choices are independent. Local video supports MKV/MP4/MOV/M4V; multi-track audio supports M4A/MKA, including the existing YouTube soundtrack source path. WAV/MP3 remain separate single-soundtrack exports. Original Audio is optionally retained as the default selectable audio track. AD audio is a described program mix, not a translation/dub of source dialogue. Player/default caption behavior varies by container; MKV/MKA provide the clearest track/accessibility metadata.

Track plans freeze explicit saved-result IDs, paired captions, presentation and formats at Save. All jobs validate before processing; validation checks current narration, readable recordings, segment duration, selected transcript/description timing and one result per language. Temporary audio/caption sources and incomplete output are removed on failure/cancellation; existing destinations commit only after validation. Auto remains Auto in filenames/titles, with undetermined container language metadata. New filenames use readable language names and stable selection identifiers. Multi-language files live under combined folders such as exports/en+hi/ and exports/auto+en/; language lists and selected-result provenance survive organization and match Created Exports/deletion filters. MKA is classified as audio on both desktops. Separate-language exports now use readable language names too.

Fixed an existing replacement-provenance defect discovered during this work: a replace-enabled batch job could relabel unrelated tracked exports. Registration now updates only newly created paths or the specifically committed replacement. Regression coverage verifies another multilingual output and its provenance remain unchanged.

## 2026-11-06 — 1.0.234: original and multilingual export track names

New exports explicitly name the original soundtrack Original Audio. AD tracks consistently use language-labelled names such as English - Audio Description and Hindi - Audio Description, with Auto remaining Auto. Caption names include language and Transcript/AD Transcript. The same naming applies with One File with Selected Tracks on or off, generated/imported narration, and supported ISO video/audio containers. Track names no longer carry version/UUID clutter; result IDs and versions remain in export provenance and filenames.

Both engines now use the same bounded portable movie-header writer before committing MOV/MP4/M4V/M4A files. It writes plain track name atoms for VLC, localized full names and extended language tags for Apple media selection, accessibility characteristics and alternate groups. Encoded media and chunk offsets are preserved; repeat application is idempotent. Known original source language tags are preserved independently of the selected translated transcript/AD language. Unknown original language remains undetermined, rather than being guessed from AD. Mac's former post-commit AVMutableMovie rewrite is replaced by read-only AVFoundation verification: it no longer limits exports to two audio tracks, truncates handler names, or relabels every track using one project language.

Player naming limitation: with an untagged source, AVFoundation's generated display name is Original Audio - Unknown language, even though the stored name is exactly Original Audio. QuickTime can append this language suffix; it cannot safely be removed by falsely assigning English or another language. Auto tracks can receive the same player-generated suffix. VLC uses the explicit names and may append known language names in brackets. This limitation is documented plainly; old exports must be exported again to get updated names, with normal replacement confirmation. Existing user project files/exports were not rewritten in this task.

## 2026-11-06 — Copy to translation enabled by default — 1.0.236

Supersedes the unchanged-copy default in 1.0.235 following the user's clarification. Copy to now enables Translate Text by default and offers Translate and Copy. Source Language accepts Auto and detects actual/mixed source text; translation requires a specific target language. Turning Translate Text off retains the local Copy workflow, including Auto and same-language duplicates. Both desktop interfaces, command guards, and shared engine behavior match. Existing Edit-menu/inspector/context/segment access, saved-version selection, Select All and Selected Segments Only remain.

The engine's translate-results command validates all requested sources before any network request, sends only selected saved text through the configured text_rewrite model, uses structured JSON responses in bounded batches, verifies exact item coverage, and commits new target versions only after every batch succeeds. ## 2026-11-07 — Millisecond video durations and segment edit bounds — 1.0.240

Local and YouTube video duration labels now consistently use HH:MM:SS.mmm: Video Information, source details in Project Settings, project list/grid/sidebar labels, YouTube lists/details and created-export video information. Widened duration table columns. The project timeline uses the same shared formatter for current time / total duration on Mac and Windows; Windows previously omitted total duration. Embedded YouTube controls remain owned by YouTube and keep their own formatting; no extra precision is inferred from whole-second metadata.

The shared Mac validator covers single and bulk edits; Save Segment and Save All also guard the action itself. Windows validates both start/end and rechecks timing before handling a dialog result. Existing overlap/order/empty-text validation remains.

## 2026-11-07 — Saved-version guidance, File deletion and responsive deletion progress — 1.0.241

Added canonical Help editor topic #saved-versions and website guide section 4.7, Understand and compare saved versions, plus FAQ and links from analysis/narration/export Help. Explains timed segments versus saved results; language/type-specific version numbering; origin labels including Mixed; creation timestamps/newest-first lists versus active selection; view-mode switching and review drafts; checking AD Overview and Play Narration; missing audio versus segment counts; Created Exports filters; legacy export associations; shared-file folders and the absence of a complete per-version file manifest in current pickers. Export choices and deletion checkboxes are explicitly distinguished. Generic examples do not embed the user's changing project inventory.

Both desktop File menus now include Delete Project… (delete-project) and Choose What to Delete… (delete-content) after Export, with matching separators, active-editor/idle/pending-summary guards and no shortcuts. The first opens the existing confirmed Bin/Recycle Bin flow; the second opens Delete Content by Language. Existing Project-menu and context actions remain. Updated both menu parity references and mutation tests; Windows native smoke expectations check both commands, gallery disabled state and deletion progress/cancellation visibility.

Whole-project recycling now runs off the UI thread (Mac detached task; Windows STA worker for shell recycling). Shared activity state shows a spinner and deletion status across All Projects/editor windows for confirmed whole-project and engine content/result/audio/export deletion. There is no fabricated percentage or Cancel Processing control for deletion. Activity clears after removal/reload or failure, and reports errors normally. Successful whole-project deletion notifies all affected editors: Mac closes matching project-editor windows and opens All Projects; Windows closes matching editors and ensures a gallery window remains. No automatic switch to another project. Failed/cancelled deletion leaves the project open; section/result deletion keeps its editor. Windows only closes editors when its deletion method reports success. Missing-folder entry removal also closes matching editors.

Production Mac asynchronous recycling ran on a newly created disposable folder: busy/progress stayed active, successful completion notified only after clearing progress and reloading, and the fixture was restored from the Bin. Three engine deletion checks passed for shared-file retention, audio-only versus segment removal and read-only preview. ## 2026-11-07 — Dialogue-free and music-video prompts — 1.0.248 / build 248

- Updated built-in Summary and Audio Descriptions system instructions and user prompts. Empty, unavailable, and non-speech-only transcript context is optional for visual understanding of dialogue-free short films, silent videos, videos without audio, and music/sound-effects-only videos. Music-video guidance covers visible performance, choreography, staging, imagery, editing, and visual narrative; it prohibits invented speech/lyrics and unsupported visual events inferred from lyrics. AD can accompany instrumental music sparsely while preserving speech, sung lyrics, important sound cues and musical moments. Transcript ranges, duration, non-overlap and natural speaking-time requirements remain enforced. Prompts request only final Markdown or JSON, without deliberation, quoted instructions, or language/transcript discussion. Required AD project context and selected-range reanalysis use matching guidance.
- Added bundled exact-default migration history. Engine settings and both native settings loaders advance only fields identical to their former built-in values; independently customized fields, empty custom instructions, and model overrides are retained. The user's current four Summary/AD fields matched the old defaults. Native inspection of the installed Mac Settings > Developer prompts > Summary / Audio Descriptions verified all four updated fields. No live Gemini call or manual project-content edit/regeneration was performed by this task; existing results need regeneration and review to adopt the prompts.

## 2026-11-07 — Story-first summaries and saved YouTube-description context — 1.0.251 / build 251

- Summary system/user prompts now begin immediately with a story/video title and the story itself. They silently verify transcript claims against audible speech, disregard unsupported ASR text from music/effects, and exclude thought labels, prompt quotations, ASR/hallucination explanations, dialogue-availability comments, source assessments, and language deliberation. Former built-in fields (including 1.0.248 and the intermediate 1.0.250 wording) migrate by exact match; custom fields remain independent. Native installed Settings > Developer prompts > Summary was inspected and shows both the story-first contract and saved YouTube-description guidance.
- Added a summary save boundary: keep finished Markdown from its top-level title onward, skip fenced examples/explicit reasoning blocks when identifying the title, accept a single Markdown wrapper, and reject unfinished title-only or reasoning-only output without replacing saved content. Read-only replay of the affected Andy project’s saved 7,224-character summary removes its 27-line preamble and preserves the final titled summary; no user result was manually edited or regenerated. This does not retrospectively clean existing saved summaries. Generate a new alternative with Regenerate Summary and review it.
- Gemini video-understanding requests set supported Interactions generation_config.thinking_summaries=none. The response adapter uses the SDK final-output helper, respects an explicitly empty helper result, and its fallback reads only trailing model-output text, skipping thought steps and prior tool-cycle drafts. Current Context7 primary SDK documentation and the locally pinned google-genai 2.22.0 types were checked; no dependency upgrade was made. Summary validation also covers preamble emitted as ordinary text in the final response, as observed in the reported project.
- YouTube Summary requests now append a nonempty saved youtubeMetadata.description as JSON reference data, even with a custom stage prompt. Local-video requests, transcription, and direct Audio Description generation do not receive this additional context. Missing/blank/invalid metadata adds nothing. Only the video description field is included, excluding account/credential/playlist metadata. No extra metadata fetch is made. Prompts use the description as secondary story/name/background context, prioritize actual video and confirmed speech, ignore embedded commands/links/promotional copy, and suppress commentary about using or lacking the description. Refresh YouTube Details updates the saved snapshot before later regeneration.
- Final source validation: Mac 123 tests pass with one existing platform skip; Windows-source 69 tests pass. New regressions exercise reported preambles, incomplete output preservation, multilingual Markdown, SDK/fallback response boundaries, no/blank/invalid/local description cases, full Unicode metadata reference data, custom prompts, and summary-only request attachment for local/YouTube stage paths. Both combined-workspace menu checkers and all 28 menu tests pass from each checkout. Website lint/TypeScript and production build pass with the existing chunk-size advisory; final guide section and privacy text render without console errors. Documentation search/navigation for dialogue-free guidance passes. Canonical Help validates 13 pages/anchors/links/images/search index; Windows Help generated. Source diffs preserve Windows platform-specific engine behavior.
- Xcode MCP project/open access verified (external agents Always). Its intermediate 1.0.250 BuildProject passed, zero errors, 49.076 s; the exact task-generated Debug app was unregistered and removed before installation. Final required script/build_and_run.sh --verify passed for 1.0.251/build 251, replacing /Applications/VidiStudio.app and verifying launch. App, Share extension and Finder extension all report 1.0.251/build 251, stable team LLVWNNUV26; previous-install designated-requirement/signature continuity passes and verify_extensions.py --registered passes. Spotlight lists only /Applications/VidiStudio.app; LaunchServices lists only that app and its embedded extensions. No extension elections, containers, or privacy protections were reset. Intermediate 1.0.250 is superseded by the final 1.0.251 installation.
- Installed packaged-engine tests via VIDISTUDIO_TEST_ENGINE=/Applications/VidiStudio.app/Contents/Resources/Engine/vidistudio-engine pass all 19 selected synthetic-media/system-speech/saved-result CLI regressions. Installed prompt defaults match source and all 13 installed Help HTML pages match generated Help. ## 2026-11-07 — 1.0.252 / build 252: expose every stored version in viewing selectors

The View saved-results menus and the shared inspector/project-settings controls now include review drafts and incomplete/empty results for the active tab and viewing language. Review drafts have a visible and accessible Review draft suffix. View Project Language includes categories containing only drafts or empty stored results. The Mac draft-only picker has an explicit Choose a result placeholder, allowing its first draft to be chosen. Export readiness remains status metadata rather than a viewing filter; export and copy source selectors retain their saved-only validation.

Explicitly selecting a draft uses Keep Both semantics: save and select that exact result without replacing any earlier version, payload, history or media. Wrong-language selection is rejected before the draft is accepted. Section generation targets remain independent. Selection is shared by menu and inspector and remains guarded while busy or with unsaved summary edits. Windows mirrors the selectors/guards and labels; status computation can inspect a draft's own audio without declaring it export ready. Updated the shared menu checker and native Swift language/result fixture.

Validation: Mac source suites tests.test_language_results, tests.test_menu_parity, tests.test_saved_result_accessibility, tests.test_multitrack_exports and tests.test_copy_translation pass: 95 tests, one existing skip. Used the repository .venv and PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/ffmpeg-full/bin:$PATH; initial system-Python run lacked certifi, and an initial media run found an ffmpeg without the ass filter. Both environment issues were resolved for the passing run. Windows-source menu/CLI suites pass 47 tests. Production Swift availability fixture and combined menu hierarchy/46-shortcut checker pass. Installed packaged-engine CLI suite passes 19 tests, including all four content kinds, draft-only languages, empty results, switching back, reopening, preservation of old records and generation targets. No live Gemini generation was needed.

Built, signed and installed /Applications/VidiStudio.app 1.0.252/build 252 with matching Share and Finder extensions, all team LLVWNNUV26 and signing continuity verified against the prior installation. Final build-and-run --verify succeeds. PlugInKit verifies one registered instance of each extension at the installed bundle; Spotlight and LaunchServices identify only /Applications/VidiStudio.app and its embedded extensions. The build product was removed by the installer. Native inspection on the user's Andy project confirms Summary Saved Results lists Version 2 (Review draft) and Version 1 (Selected, Export ready). No user draft was accepted or content edited for this inspection. The screen locked before a native View submenu click, so that native interaction remains unverified; menu contract, compiled native menu accessibility and installed-engine selection were checked independently. Native Help Viewer rendering/search was not repeated.

Canonical Help/links/search generated and verified for 13 Mac pages, installed with the final signed build, and 12 matching Windows pages generated. Website guide/FAQ updated; npm run lint/build pass with the existing bundle-size advisory. Local in-app browser verifies saved-versions search and navigation to #docs-4-7-saved-versions, new draft/all-version guidance, absence of the old exclusion text, and no console errors. Website sources/local production build updated, not published. Windows remains version-synchronized at 1.0.252/package 1.0.252.0; native build/install/UI blocked because prlctl start reports the command requires Parallels Pro or Business. No commit or push. Existing unrelated summary/prompt work was preserved.
